Law published: Žin. 2000, No. 89-2741,
i. k. 1001010ISTAIII-1968
OF THE REPUBLIC OF LITHUANIA
LAW ON THE APPROVAL AND ENTRY INTO FORCE OF THE CRIMINAL CODE
26 September 2000 No. VIII-1968 Vilnius

Article. Approval of the Criminal Code of the Republic of Lithuania

By this Law, the Seimas approves the Criminal Code of the Republic of Lithuania.

Article. Entry into force of the Criminal Code of the Republic of Lithuania

1.	The Criminal Code of the Republic of Lithuania shall enter into force together and only when harmonised with the new Code of Criminal Procedure of the Republic of Lithuania and the Penal Enforcement Code of the Republic of Lithuania.
2.	The specific date of entry into force of all the codes referred to in paragraph 1 of this Article shall be established by a separate law.
Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-1168, 2002-10-31, Žin., 2002, No. 112-4973 (2002-11-22)

Article. Procedure for implementation of the Criminal Code of the Republic of Lithuania

The procedure for implementation of the Criminal Code of the Republic of Lithuania shall be established by a separate law.
I promulgate this Law adopted by the Seimas of the Republic of Lithuania.

PRESIDENT OF THE REPUBLIC	VALDAS ADAMKUS

APPROVED
by Law No. VIII-1968 of 26 September 2000

CRIMINAL CODE
OF THE REPUBLIC OF LITHUANIA
GENERAL PART I CHAPTER
GENERAL PROVISIONS

Article. Purpose of the Criminal Code of the Republic of Lithuania

1.	The Criminal Code of the Republic of Lithuania is a unified criminal law, the purpose of which is to protect human and citizen rights and freedoms, the interests of society and the state from criminal acts by means of criminal law.
2.	This Code:
1)	defines which acts are crimes and criminal misdemeanours and prohibits them;
2)	establishes penalties, measures of criminal and educational influence for acts provided for in this Code, and compulsory medical measures;
3)	establishes the grounds and conditions for criminal liability, as well as the grounds and conditions under which persons who have committed criminal acts may be released from criminal liability or punishment.
3.	The provisions of this Code are harmonised with the provisions of the legal acts of the European Union referred to in the Annex to this Code.
Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-2169, 2004-04-27, Žin., 2004, No. 72-2492 (2004-04-30)

Article. Basic provisions of criminal liability

1.	A person shall be liable under this Code only if the act committed by him was prohibited by the criminal law in force at the time of commission of the criminal act.
2.	Ignorance of the law does not release from criminal liability.
3.	A person shall be liable under criminal law only if he is guilty of committing a criminal act and only if, at the time of commission of the act, lawful conduct could have been required of him.
4.	Only a person whose act corresponds to the elements of a crime or criminal misdemeanour provided for by criminal law shall be liable under criminal law.
5.	Penalties, measures of criminal or educational influence, and compulsory medical measures shall be imposed only in accordance with the law.
6.	No one may be punished for the same criminal act a second time.

II	CHAPTER
VALIDITY OF CRIMINAL LAW

Article. Temporal validity of criminal law

1.	The criminality of an act and the punishability of a person shall be determined by the criminal law in force at the time of commission of that act. The time of commission of a criminal act is the time of action (inaction) or the time of occurrence of the consequences provided for by criminal law, if the person intended the consequences to occur at a different time.
2.	A criminal law that eliminates the criminality of an act, mitigates punishment or otherwise alleviates the legal situation of a person who has committed a criminal act shall have retroactive effect,
i.e. it shall apply to persons who committed a criminal act before such law entered into force, as well as to persons serving a sentence and persons with a criminal record.
3.	A criminal law that establishes the criminality of an act, increases punishment or otherwise aggravates the legal situation of a person who has committed a criminal act shall not have retroactive effect.

An exception is constituted by the provisions of this Code establishing liability for genocide (Article 99), conduct with persons prohibited by international law (Article 100), killing of persons protected by international humanitarian law (Article 101), deportation or transfer of civilians (Article 102), injury, torture or other inhuman treatment of persons protected by international humanitarian law or violation of protection of their property (Article 103), forced use of civilians or prisoners of war in the enemy's armed forces (Article 105), destruction of protected objects or looting of national values (Article 106), aggression (Article 110), prohibited war attack (Article 111), use of prohibited means of warfare (Article 112), negligent performance of commander's duties (Article 1131).
Note. It is recognised that Article 3(3) of the Criminal Code of the Republic of Lithuania (version of 22 March 2011; Žin., 2011, No. 38-1805), insofar as the legal regulation established therein, according to which a person may be tried under Article 99 of the Criminal Code for acts aimed at physically destroying all or part of people belonging to any social or political group, committed before liability for genocide of people belonging to a social or political group was established in the Criminal Code, is in conflict with Article 31(4) of the Constitution of the Republic of Lithuania and the constitutional principle of the rule of law.
Constitutional Court of the Republic of Lithuania, Ruling
No. KT11-N4/2014, 2014-03-18, published TAR 2014-03-19,
i. k. 2014-03226

4.	The measures of criminal or educational influence and compulsory medical measures that are provided for by the criminal law in force at the time of adoption of the court decision shall be imposed. Extended confiscation of property shall be imposed only if it was provided for in the criminal law in force at the time of commission of the prohibited act under this Code.
Amendments to the Article:
No. XI-1199, 2010-12-02, Žin., 2010, No. 145-7439 (2010-12-11)
No. XI-1291, 2011-03-22, Žin., 2011, No. 38-1805 (2011-03-31)

Article

Validity of criminal law for persons who have committed criminal acts in the territory of the Lithuanian State or on ships or aircraft bearing the flag or identification marks of the Lithuanian State
1.	Persons who have committed criminal acts in the territory of the Lithuanian State or on ships or aircraft bearing the flag or identification marks of the Lithuanian State shall be liable under this Code.
2.	The place of commission of a criminal act is the place where the person acted or was required and able to act, or the place where the consequences provided for by criminal law occurred. The place of commission of a criminal act by accomplices is the place where that act was committed, and if one of the accomplices acted in another place – the place of his action.
3.	Where the same criminal act has been committed both in the territory of the Lithuanian State and abroad, that act shall be deemed to have been committed in the territory of the Lithuanian State if it was commenced, completed or interrupted in that territory.
4.	The issue of criminal liability of persons who, under the norms of international law, enjoy immunity from criminal jurisdiction and have committed a criminal act in the territory of the Lithuanian State shall be resolved in accordance with the international treaties of the Republic of Lithuania and this Code.
Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, No. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)

Article

Criminal liability of citizens of the Republic of Lithuania and other persons permanently residing in Lithuania for crimes committed abroad
Citizens of the Republic of Lithuania and other persons permanently residing in Lithuania shall be liable under this Code for crimes committed abroad.

Article

Criminal liability of foreigners for crimes committed abroad against the Lithuanian State
Foreigners who do not have a permanent place of residence in the Republic of Lithuania shall be liable under the criminal law if they have committed abroad crimes against the Lithuanian State provided for in Articles 114–128 of this Code.

Article. Criminal liability for crimes provided for in international treaties

Persons shall be liable under this Code, irrespective of their citizenship and place of residence, as well as the place of commission of the crime and whether the act committed is punishable under the laws of the place where the crime was committed, when they commit crimes for which liability is provided for on the basis of international treaties:
1) crimes against humanity and war crimes (Articles 99–1131);
2) trafficking in human beings (Article 147);
3) purchase or sale of a child (Article 157);
4) manufacture, possession or distribution of counterfeit money or securities

(Article 213);

5) legalisation of criminally obtained property (Article 216);
6) bribery (Article 225);
7) trading in influence (Article 226);
8) corruption (Article 227);
9) piracy (Article 2511);
10) terrorist and terrorism-related crimes (Article 2521, paragraphs 1 and 2);
11) unlawful conduct with nuclear or radioactive materials or other sources of ionising radiation (Articles 256, 2561 and 257);
12) crimes related to handling narcotic or psychotropic, poisonous or potent substances (Articles 259–269);
13) crimes against the environment (Articles 270, 2701, 2702, 2703, 271, 272, 274).

Article. Criminal liability for crimes committed abroad

1. A person who has committed abroad crimes provided for in Articles 5 and 6 of this Code shall be liable under the criminal law only if the act committed is recognised as a crime and is punishable under the criminal law of the State where the crime was committed and the Criminal Code of the Republic of Lithuania. If a person who has committed a crime abroad is tried in the Republic of Lithuania, but different penalties are provided for this crime in both States, the penalty for the person who committed the crime shall be imposed in accordance with the laws of the Republic of Lithuania, but it may not exceed the maximum amount established in the criminal laws of the State where the crime was committed.
2. A person who has committed crimes provided for in Articles 5, 6 and 7 of the Criminal Code of the Republic of Lithuania shall not be liable under this Code if he or she:
1) has served the sentence imposed by a foreign State court;
2) has been released from serving all or part of the sentence imposed by a foreign State court;
3) has been acquitted by a foreign State court judgment, released from criminal liability or punishment, or the punishment has not been imposed due to the statute of limitations or other legal grounds provided for in that State.
3. A citizen of the Republic of Lithuania or another person permanently residing in Lithuania who has committed abroad one or more crimes provided for in Articles 129, 135, 138, 148, 149, 150, 151, 1511,
1521, 153 of this Code, in paragraph 1 of Article 162, in paragraph 3 of Article 307, in paragraph 3 of Article 308, in paragraphs 2 and 3 of Article 309

shall be punished irrespective of whether the act committed is punishable under the criminal law of the State where the crime was committed.

Amendments to the Article:

Article. Extradition

1.	A citizen of the Republic of Lithuania who has committed a criminal act in the Republic of Lithuania or in the territory of another state may be extradited to a foreign state or surrendered to the International Criminal Court only on the basis of an international treaty of the Republic of Lithuania or a resolution of the United Nations Security Council.
2.	A foreigner who has committed a criminal act in the Republic of Lithuania or in the territory of another state shall be extradited to the respective state or surrendered to the International Criminal Court only on the basis of an international treaty of the Republic of Lithuania or a resolution of the United Nations Security Council.
3.	A citizen of the Republic of Lithuania or a foreigner may not be extradited if:
1)	the act committed is not considered a crime or criminal misdemeanour under this Code;
2)	the criminal act was committed in the territory of the Lithuanian state;
3)	the person is being prosecuted for a crime of a political nature;
4)	the person was convicted, acquitted or released from criminal liability or punishment for the criminal act committed;
5)	the death penalty may be imposed on the person for the crime committed in another state;
6)	the limitation period for the adoption or execution of a conviction has expired;
7)	the person has been released from punishment under an act of amnesty or by way of pardon;
8)	there are other grounds provided for in international treaties of the Republic of Lithuania.
4.	Persons who have been granted asylum or temporary protection under the laws of the Republic of Lithuania shall not be punished under the Criminal Law of the Republic of Lithuania and shall not be extradited to foreign states for criminal acts for which they were persecuted abroad, except in the cases provided for in Article 7 of this Code.
Article part amendments:
No. XII-1398, 2014-12-09, published TAR 2014-12-18,

Article 9(1). Surrender of a person under a European arrest warrant
1.	On the basis of a European arrest warrant, a citizen of the Republic of Lithuania or a foreigner suspected of committing a criminal act in the state that issued the European arrest warrant or sentenced in that state to a custodial sentence but who has not served it shall be surrendered to the state that issued the European arrest warrant.
2.	A citizen of the Republic of Lithuania or a foreigner shall be surrendered under a European arrest warrant only if, under the laws of the state that issued the warrant, a custodial sentence of at least one year is provided for the criminal act committed, and if the European arrest warrant was issued for the execution of an already imposed custodial sentence – only if the term of the imposed sentence is not shorter than four months.
3.	A citizen of the Republic of Lithuania or a foreigner shall not be surrendered to the state that issued the European arrest warrant if:
1)	the court determines that the surrender of the person under the European arrest warrant would violate fundamental human rights and (or) freedoms;
2)	the person has been released from serving the punishment for the criminal act committed for which the European arrest warrant was issued by an act of amnesty or by way of pardon in the Republic of Lithuania;
3)	the person was convicted in the Republic of Lithuania or in another Member State of the European Union for the criminal act for which the European arrest warrant was issued and the imposed sentence has been served, is being served or can no longer be executed under the laws of the convicting state;

4)	at the time of committing the criminal act, the person had not reached the age from which criminal liability is possible under the criminal laws of the Republic of Lithuania for the act committed;
5)	the person was acquitted or released from criminal liability or punishment in the Republic of Lithuania or in another Member State of the European Union for the criminal act for which the European arrest warrant was issued.

5.	If the European arrest warrant was issued for the execution of a custodial sentence imposed on a citizen of the Republic of Lithuania or a foreigner and he or she did not personally participate in the examination of the case in court and a decision was made to impose a custodial sentence, the citizen of the Republic of Lithuania or the foreigner may not be surrendered, except in cases where the European arrest warrant indicates that in accordance with the procedural requirements established in the national legislation of the Member State of the European Union that issued the European arrest warrant:
1)	the person was personally served with a summons to court in due time or the person received official information by other means about the scheduled time and place of the examination of the case in court in such a way that it is obvious that he or she knew about the scheduled examination of the case in court, and the person was also informed that a decision may be made even if he or she does not appear for the examination of the case in court, or
2)	the person, knowing about the scheduled examination of the case in court, authorised a defence counsel of his or her choice or appointed by the state to defend him or her during the examination of the case in court and that defence counsel actually defended this person during the examination of the case in court, or
3)	after the decision was served on the person and he or she was clearly informed about the right to a retrial or the right to lodge an appeal, during the examination of which this person has the right to participate and which provide an opportunity to re-examine the case, including new evidence, on the merits, and following this process the initial decision may be set aside, the person clearly indicated that he or she does not contest the decision, or did not request a retrial or did not lodge an appeal within the established period, or
4)	in the event that the decision was not personally served on the person, but the European arrest warrant confirms that after the surrender of the person this decision will be immediately served personally and this person will be clearly informed about the right to a retrial or the right to lodge an appeal, during the examination of which this person will have the right to participate and which provide an opportunity to re-examine the case, including new evidence, on the merits, and following this process the initial decision may be set aside, and that the person will be informed about the period within which he or she must request a retrial or lodge an appeal, as indicated in the European arrest warrant.
6.	It is not possible to refuse to surrender a person under a European arrest warrant issued for a criminal act related to taxes, levies, customs duties, currency exchange, on the grounds that taxes or levies of the same type are not established in the legal acts of the Republic of Lithuania, or on the grounds that the legal regulation of such taxes, levies, customs duties or currency exchange in the Republic of Lithuania differs from the legal regulation established in the legal acts of the state that issued the European arrest warrant.

7.	If the European arrest warrant was issued for a criminal act for which the laws of the state that issued the European arrest warrant provide for a life imprisonment sentence, a citizen of the Republic of Lithuania or a foreigner shall be surrendered on condition that the laws of the state that issued the European arrest warrant provide for the possibility for the convicted person to apply for release from punishment or its mitigation no later than after serving twenty years of imprisonment.

8.	If the European arrest warrant was issued for the purpose of criminal prosecution, a citizen of the Republic of Lithuania or a person permanently residing in the Republic of Lithuania may be surrendered on condition that the person in respect of whom the state that issued the European arrest warrant adopted a judgment will be returned to the Republic of Lithuania to serve the custodial sentence imposed on him or her, if so requested by the surrendered person or if so demanded by the Prosecutor General's Office of the Republic of Lithuania. This condition shall not apply if one or more grounds specified in the Law of the Republic of Lithuania "On Mutual Recognition and Execution of Decisions in Criminal Matters of Member States of the European Union" are established in advance on the basis of which the Republic of Lithuania could refuse to take over the execution of the imposed custodial sentence.

III	CHAPTER
CRIME AND CRIMINAL MISDEMEANOUR

This excerpt sits under: Consolidated version from 2024-01-01

Article. Types of Criminal Acts

Criminal acts are divided into crimes and criminal misdemeanours.

Article. Crime

1.	A crime is a dangerous act (action or omission) prohibited by this Code for which a custodial sentence is prescribed.
2.	Crimes are intentional and negligent. Intentional crimes are divided into minor, medium, serious and very serious.
3.	A minor crime is an intentional crime for which the maximum penalty prescribed by the criminal law does not exceed three years of imprisonment.
4.	A medium crime is an intentional crime for which the maximum penalty prescribed by the criminal law exceeds three years of imprisonment but does not exceed six years of imprisonment.
5.	A serious crime is an intentional crime for which the maximum penalty prescribed by the criminal law exceeds six years of imprisonment but does not exceed ten years of imprisonment.
6.	A very serious crime is an intentional crime for which the maximum penalty prescribed by the criminal law exceeds ten years of imprisonment.

Article. Criminal Misdemeanour

A criminal misdemeanour is a dangerous act (action or omission) prohibited by this Code for which a penalty not involving deprivation of liberty is prescribed, except for arrest.

Article. Age from which a Person is Liable under Criminal Law

1.	A person who had reached the age of sixteen years before committing a crime or criminal misdemeanour is liable under this Code, and in the cases provided for in paragraph 2 of this Article – fourteen years.
2.	A person who had reached the age of fourteen years before committing a crime or criminal misdemeanour is liable for murder (Article 129), serious bodily harm (Article 135), rape (Article 149), sexual assault (Article 150), theft (Article 178), robbery (Article 180), extortion of property (Article 181), destruction or damage of property (Article 187, paragraph 2), seizure of firearms, ammunition, explosives or explosive substances (Article 254), theft, extortion or other unlawful taking of narcotic or psychotropic substances (Article 263), damage to vehicles or roads and equipment thereon (Article 280, paragraph 2).
3.	A person who had not reached the age of fourteen years before committing a dangerous act provided for in this Code may be subject to educational measures or other measures in the manner established by the laws of the Republic of Lithuania.
Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-1992, 2004-01-29, Žin., 2004, No. 25-760 (2004-02-14)

Article. Forms of Guilt

A person is found guilty of committing a crime or criminal misdemeanour if he or she committed that act intentionally or through negligence.

Article. Intentional Crime and Criminal Misdemeanour

1.	A crime or criminal misdemeanour is intentional if it was committed with direct or indirect intent.

2.	A crime or criminal misdemeanour is committed with direct intent if:
1)	in committing it the person was aware of the dangerous nature of the criminal act and wanted to act in that way;
2)	in committing it the person was aware of the dangerous nature of the criminal act, foresaw that his or her action or omission could give rise to the consequences provided for in this Code, and wanted them to occur.
3.	A crime or criminal misdemeanour is committed with indirect intent if, in committing it, the person was aware of the dangerous nature of the criminal act, foresaw that his or her action or omission could give rise to the consequences provided for in this Code, and although he or she did not want them to occur, consciously allowed them to occur.

Article. Negligent Crime and Criminal Misdemeanour

1.	A crime or criminal misdemeanour is negligent if it was committed through criminal overconfidence or criminal carelessness.
2.	A crime or criminal misdemeanour is committed through criminal overconfidence if the person who committed it foresaw that his or her action or omission could give rise to the consequences provided for in this Code, but frivolously hoped to avoid them.
3.	A crime or criminal misdemeanour is committed through criminal carelessness if the person who committed it did not foresee that his or her action or omission could give rise to the consequences provided for in this Code, although according to the circumstances of the act and his or her personal characteristics he or she could and should have foreseen it.
4.	A person is punished for committing a crime or criminal misdemeanour through negligence only in cases separately provided for in the Special Part of this Code.

Article. Non-Imputability

1.	A person is non-imputable if, when committing an act prohibited by this Code, he or she, due to a mental disorder, was unable to understand its dangerousness or to control his or her actions.
2.	A person found by the court to be non-imputable is not liable under this Code for a dangerous act committed. The court may apply to him or her the compulsory medical measures provided for in Article 98 of this Code.

Article. Diminished Imputability

1.	A person is found by the court to be of diminished imputability if, when committing an act prohibited by this Code, that person, due to a mental disorder which is not sufficient grounds to find him or her non-imputable, was unable to fully understand the dangerous nature of the criminal act or to control his or her actions.
2.	A person who has committed a criminal misdemeanour, a negligent or a minor or medium intentional crime and is found by the court to be of diminished imputability is liable under the criminal law, but the penalty may be mitigated on the basis of Article 59 of this Code, or he or she may be released from criminal liability and the measures of criminal impact provided for in Article 67 of this Code or the compulsory medical measures provided for in Article 98 of this Code may be applied to him or her.
3.	A person who has committed a serious or very serious crime and is found by the court to be of diminished imputability is liable under the criminal law, but the penalty may be mitigated on the basis of Article 59 of this Code.

Article

Liability of a Person for a Criminal Act Committed while Intoxicated by Alcohol, Narcotic, Psychotropic or Other Psychoactive Substances
1.	A person who committed a criminal act while intoxicated by alcohol, narcotic, psychotropic or other psychoactive substances is not released from criminal liability.
2.	A person who committed a criminal misdemeanour, a negligent or a minor or medium intentional crime while being intoxicated or drugged against his or her will and therefore not fully able to understand the dangerous nature of the criminal act or to control his or her actions is released from criminal liability.

3.	A person who, under the conditions specified in paragraph 2 of this Article, committed a serious or very serious crime is liable under the criminal law, but the penalty may be mitigated on the basis of Article 59 of this Code.

This excerpt sits under: Consolidated version as of 2024-01-01

Article. Criminal liability of a legal person

1.	A legal person shall be liable only for criminal acts for the commission of which the liability of a legal person is provided for in the Special Part of this Code.
2.	A legal person shall be liable for criminal acts committed by a natural person only in the event that the criminal act was committed for the benefit or in the interests of the legal person by a natural person acting individually or on behalf of the legal person, if that person, while holding a managerial position in the legal person, had the right to:
1)	represent the legal person, or
2)	make decisions on behalf of the legal person, or
3)	control the activities of the legal person.
3.	A legal person may also be liable for criminal acts if they were committed for the benefit of the legal person by an employee or authorised representative of the legal person on the instruction or with the permission of the person referred to in paragraph 2 of this Article, or due to inadequate supervision or control.
4.	A legal person may be liable for criminal acts committed under the conditions specified in paragraph 2 or 3 of this Article by another legal person which it controls or which represents it, if those acts were committed for the benefit of the first-mentioned legal person on the instruction or with the permission of a person holding a managerial position in it or authorised by it, or due to inadequate supervision or control.
5.	The criminal liability of a legal person shall not exclude the criminal liability of a natural person who committed, organised, incited or aided in the commission of a criminal act. The criminal liability of a legal person for a criminal act committed, organised, incited or aided in commission for its benefit or in its interests by a natural person shall not be excluded by the criminal liability of the natural person, nor by the fact that the natural person is released from criminal liability for that act or is not prosecuted for other reasons.
6.	Under this Code, the State, a municipality, a State or municipal institution or establishment, and an international public organisation shall not be liable. State and municipal institutions and establishments shall not include, and shall be liable under this Code, State and municipal enterprises, as well as public institutions whose owner or shareholder is the State or a municipality, and public limited liability companies and private limited liability companies all or part of whose shares are owned by the State or a municipality. Amendments to the paragraph of the Article:
No. XIII-391, 2017-06-01, published TAR 2017-06-02,
i. k. 2017-09472

Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-2314, 2004-07-05, Žin., 2004, No. 108-4030 (2004-07-13)
Amendments to the Article:
No. XII-2780, 2016-11-10, published TAR 2016-11-16,
i. k. 2016-26876

CHAPTER IV
STAGES AND FORMS OF A CRIMINAL ACT

Article. Preparation to commit a crime

1.	Preparation to commit a crime shall be the search for or adaptation of means and instruments, the drawing up of a plan of action, the gathering of accomplices, or other intentional creation of conditions facilitating the commission of a crime. A person shall be liable only for preparation to commit a serious or very serious crime.
2.	A person shall be liable for preparation to commit a crime under paragraph 1 of this Article and the Article of this Code which provides for the corresponding completed crime. The punishment for such a person may be mitigated on the basis of Article 62 of this Code.

Article. Attempt to commit a criminal act

1.	An attempt to commit a criminal act shall be an intentional act or omission by which the commission of a crime or misdemeanour is directly commenced, if the act was not completed due to circumstances beyond the control of the offender.

2.	An attempt to commit a criminal act shall also exist where the offender does not realise that he cannot complete the act because he is attempting an improper object or is using improper means.
3.	A person shall be liable for an attempt to commit a criminal act under paragraph 1 or 2 of this Article and the Article of this Code which provides for the corresponding completed crime. The punishment for such a person may be mitigated on the basis of Article 62 of this Code.

Article. Voluntary abandonment of completion of a criminal act

1.	Voluntary abandonment of completion of a crime or misdemeanour shall exist where a person of his own accord discontinues a criminal act which has been commenced, realising that he can complete it.
2.	A person who has voluntarily abandoned completion of a crime or misdemeanour shall be liable under this Code only in the event that the act committed contains the elements of another crime or misdemeanour.
3.	Where a criminal act is committed by several persons, an organiser or inciter who has voluntarily abandoned its completion shall not be liable under this Code if he took all measures within his power to prevent the accomplices from committing the criminal act which he organised or incited, and that act was not committed or its consequences did not occur. Furthermore, an aider shall not be liable under this Code if he voluntarily refused to participate in the criminal act, informed the other accomplices or law enforcement institutions thereof, and that act was not committed or it was committed without his assistance.
4.	A person who attempted to voluntarily abandon completion of a crime or misdemeanour but failed to avoid the criminal act or its consequences shall be liable under the criminal law, but his punishment may be mitigated on the basis of Article 59 of this Code. Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, No. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)

Article. Complicity and types of accomplices

1.	Complicity shall be the intentional joint participation of two or more culpable persons who have reached the age specified in Article 13 of this Code and who have agreed among themselves in the commission of a criminal act.
2.	Accomplices to a criminal act shall be the perpetrator, the organiser, the inciter and the aider.
3.	A perpetrator shall be a person who has committed a criminal act himself or by using non-culpable persons or persons who have not reached the age specified in Article 13 of this Code, or other persons who are not guilty of that act. Where a criminal act has been committed by several persons together, each of them shall be deemed a perpetrator (co-perpetrator).
4.	An organiser shall be a person who has formed an organised group or a criminal association, led or coordinated them or the activities of their members, or prepared a criminal act or led it.
5.	An inciter shall be a person who has induced another person to commit a criminal act.
6.	An aider shall be a person who has aided in the commission of a criminal act by giving advice, instructions, providing means or removing obstacles, guarding or covering for other accomplices, having promised in advance to conceal the offender, the instruments or means of commission of the criminal act, the traces of that act or objects acquired by criminal means, as well as a person who has promised in advance to realise objects acquired or produced from the criminal act.

Article. Forms of complicity

1.	Forms of complicity shall be a group of accomplices, an organised group, a criminal association.
2.	A group of accomplices shall exist where, at any stage of a criminal act, two or more persons agree to commit, continue or complete a criminal act, if at least two of them are perpetrators.
3.	An organised group shall exist where, at any stage of a criminal act, two or more persons agree to commit several crimes or one moderately serious, serious or very serious crime, and each member of the group, in committing the crime, performs a specific task or has a different role.
4.	A criminal association shall exist where three or more persons unite for joint criminal activity – for the commission of one or more moderately serious, serious or very serious crimes – who are linked by permanent mutual ties and a distribution of roles or tasks. An anti-constitutional group or organisation and a terrorist group shall be equivalent to a criminal association.

Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, No. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)
No. XII-497, 2013-07-02, Žin., 2013, No. 75-3768 (2013-07-13)

intent.

Article. Criminal liability of accomplices

1. Accomplices are liable only for those criminal acts committed by the perpetrator that were covered by their intent.

2. If the perpetrator's criminal act was interrupted at the stage of preparation or attempt, the organiser, instigator and aider are liable for preparation or attempt to commit a criminal act as accomplices.
3. If there are circumstances excluding, mitigating or aggravating criminal liability in respect of one of the accomplices, they shall not be taken into account when deciding on the criminal liability of other accomplices.
4. The organiser, instigator or aider is liable under the article of this Code providing for liability for the act committed by the perpetrator, and under Part 4, 5 or 6 of Article 24 of this Code.
5. Participants in a criminal association, regardless of their role in committing a criminal act covered by their intent, are liable under Article 249 of this Code as perpetrators.

Article. Recidivism of crimes

1. Recidivism of crimes exists when a person who has already been convicted of an intentional crime committed when he was an adult, and if the conviction for it has not expired or been cancelled in the manner established by law, again commits one or more intentional crimes. Such a person is a recidivist.
Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XII-1554, 2015-03-19, published TAR 2015-03-23,
i. k. 2015-04087

2. Recidivism of crimes is dangerous, and a person who has committed crimes may be recognised by the court as a dangerous recidivist if that person:
1) having an unexpired conviction for a very serious crime, commits a new very serious crime;
2) being a recidivist, commits a new very serious crime;
3) being a recidivist, where at least one of the crimes constituting recidivism is very serious, commits a new serious crime;
4) having three convictions for serious crimes, commits a new serious crime.
3. The court, when adopting a conviction for the last crime, taking into account the offender's personality, the degree of implementation of criminal intentions, the nature of participation in committing crimes and other circumstances of the case, may recognise the person as a dangerous recidivist.
4. The court, when deciding on the recognition of a person as a dangerous recidivist, shall not take into account convictions for crimes committed by the person when he was younger than eighteen years of age, negligent crimes, crimes for which the conviction has expired or been cancelled, as well as crimes committed abroad in the cases provided for in Part 9 of Article 97 of this Code.
5. The recognition of a person as a dangerous recidivist ceases to be valid when the person's conviction expires or is cancelled.
Amendments to the Article:
No. XII-776, 2014-03-13, published TAR 2014-03-24,
i. k. 2014-03404

CHAPTER V
CIRCUMSTANCES EXCLUDING CRIMINAL LIABILITY

Article. Necessary defence

1. A person has the right to necessary defence. He may exercise this right regardless of whether he could have avoided the encroachment or sought help from other persons or authorities.
2. A person is not liable under this Code if, without exceeding the limits of necessary defence, he committed an act formally corresponding to the elements of a crime or misdemeanour provided for in the criminal law while defending himself or another person, property, inviolability of dwelling, other rights, interests of society or the state against a dangerous encroachment that has begun or is directly threatening.

3. The limits of necessary defence are exceeded in the case where a person is intentionally killed or seriously injured, if the defence clearly did not correspond to the nature and dangerousness of the encroachment. An act committed due to great confusion or fear caused by a dangerous encroachment, or while defending against intrusion into a dwelling, is not considered to be exceeding the limits of necessary defence.
4. A person who has exceeded the limits of necessary defence is liable under the criminal law, but the punishment may be mitigated on the basis of Article 62 of this Code.

Article. Apprehension of a person who has committed a criminal act

1. A person is not liable under this Code for actions when, while pursuing, stopping, preventing escape or by other actions, he causes property damage, minor injury to health or serious injury to health through negligence to a person who has committed a criminal act and is actively trying to avoid apprehension, and when apprehending at the scene of the crime a person who has intentionally killed or attempted to kill – serious injury to health, if the person who committed the criminal act could not otherwise be apprehended.
2. The rules of necessary defence established in Article 28 of this Code apply to the act of a person repelling the resistance of a person who has committed a criminal act.

Article. Performance of professional duties

1. A person is not liable under this Code for harm caused in the performance of professional duties if he did not exceed the powers established by laws or other legal acts.
2. A person is liable under this Code for harm caused in the performance of professional duties if he exceeded the powers established by laws or other legal acts, but the punishment may be mitigated on the basis of Article 59 of this Code.

Article. Necessity

1. A person is not liable under criminal laws for an act committed in order to eliminate a danger threatening himself, other persons or their rights, interests of society or the state, if this danger could not be eliminated by other means and the harm caused is less than that which was sought to be avoided.
2. A person who by his actions has created a dangerous situation may rely on the provisions of necessity only if the dangerous situation was created through negligence.
3. A person may not justify non-performance of a duty by the provisions of necessity if, due to his profession, duties or other circumstances, he is obliged to act in conditions of greater danger.

Article. Performance of a law enforcement agency's task

1. A person is not liable under this Code if he lawfully performed actions imitating a criminal act.
2. A person is not liable under the criminal law if he participated in the activities of a criminal association or organised group and in the criminal acts committed by it while performing another lawful task of a law enforcement agency and did not exceed the limits of this task.
3. If, while performing actions imitating a criminal act or performing another task of a law enforcement agency, a person exceeded the limits of this task, he is liable under the criminal law, but the punishment may be mitigated on the basis of Article 59 of this Code.
4. Law enforcement agencies are the police, other pre-trial investigation and prosecution institutions, as well as criminal intelligence subjects.
Amendments to the Article:
No. XI-2241, 2012-10-02, Žin., 2012, No. 122-6100 (2012-10-20)

Article. Execution of an order

1. A person is not liable under the criminal law for an act committed in execution of a lawful order, directive or instruction.
2. A person is liable under the criminal law if he executed a knowingly criminal order, directive or instruction.
3. A person who refused to execute a criminal order, directive or instruction is not liable under the criminal law. Such a person may be liable under this Code only if the act committed contains the elements of another criminal act.

Article. Justified professional or economic risk

1. A person is not liable under this Code for actions as a result of which, although consequences provided for by the criminal law arise, they were performed under justified professional or economic risk for a purpose beneficial to society.
2. Risk is considered justified if the act committed complies with modern science and technology, and the specified purpose could not be achieved by actions not involving risk, and the person who took the risk took the necessary safety measures to protect legally protected interests from harm.

Article. Scientific Experiment

1. A person who caused damage while conducting a lawful scientific experiment shall not be liable under this Code if the experiment was based on scientifically approved methodologies, the problem being addressed is of exceptional importance to science, and the experimenter took necessary measures to avoid harm to interests protected by law.
2. Scientific experimentation is prohibited without the free consent of an informed experiment participant regarding possible consequences.
3. Scientific experimentation is prohibited, except in cases provided by law, with a pregnant woman, her fetus, a minor, a person with mental disorders, and a person deprived of liberty.
VI CHAPTER
RELEASE FROM CRIMINAL LIABILITY

Article

Release from Criminal Liability When a Person or Criminal Act Has Lost Its Dangerousness
A person who has committed a criminal act shall be released from criminal liability if the court finds that before the examination of the case in court, this person or the act committed by him has become non-dangerous due to a change in circumstances.

Article

Release from Criminal Liability Due to the Minor Significance of a Crime
A person who has committed a crime may be released from criminal liability by the court if, due to the extent of damage caused, the object of the crime or other peculiarities of the crime's characteristics, the act is deemed to be of minor significance.
Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, No. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)

Article

Release from Criminal Liability When the Offender and the Victim Reconcile
1. A person who has committed a misdemeanor, a negligent crime or a minor or less serious intentional crime may be released from criminal liability by the court if:
1) he confessed to having committed a criminal act and
2) voluntarily compensated or remedied the damage caused to a natural or legal person or agreed on the compensation or remedy of this damage, and
3) reconciled with the victim or a representative of a legal person or state institution,

and;

4) there is reason to believe that he will not commit new criminal acts.
2. A dangerous recidivist, as well as a person who has previously been released from criminal liability as having reconciled with the victim, if less than four years have passed from the date of reconciliation until the commission of a new act, may not be released from criminal liability on the grounds provided in paragraph 1 of this Article.
Amendments to the paragraph of the Article:
No. XII-1554, 2015-03-19, published TAR 2015-03-23,
i. k. 2015-04087

3. If a person released from criminal liability under paragraph 1 of this Article commits a misdemeanor or negligent crime within one year or fails to comply with the terms and procedure of the court-approved agreement on compensation for damage without justifiable reasons, the court may revoke the decision on release from criminal liability and decide on the criminal liability of such person for all criminal acts committed.
4. If a person released from criminal liability under paragraph 1 of this Article commits a new intentional crime within one year, the previous decision to release from criminal liability shall cease to be valid and the criminal liability of such person for all criminal acts committed shall be decided.
Amendments to the Article:
No. X-1233, 2007-06-28, Žin., 2007, No. 81-3309 (2007-07-21)
No. XI-677, 2010-02-11, Žin., 2010, No. 20-927 (2010-02-18)

Article

Release from Criminal Liability When There Are Mitigating Circumstances
A person who has committed a misdemeanor, a negligent crime or a minor intentional crime may be released from criminal liability by a reasoned decision of the court if:
1) he committed a criminal act for the first time and
2) there are at least two mitigating circumstances provided for in paragraph 1 of Article 59 of this Code, and
3) there are no aggravating circumstances.
Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, No. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)
Amendments to the Article:
No. XII-1554, 2015-03-19, published TAR 2015-03-23,
i. k. 2015-04087

39(1) Article. Release from Criminal Liability When a Person Actively Assisted in Disclosing Criminal Acts Committed by Members of an Organized Group or Criminal Association
1. A person suspected of having participated in an organized group or criminal association in committing criminal acts or of having belonged to a criminal association may be released from criminal liability if he confessed to having participated in committing such a criminal act or having belonged to a criminal association and actively assisted in disclosing criminal acts committed by members of the organized group or criminal association.
2. Paragraph 1 of this Article shall not apply to a person who participated in intentional killing or who has already been released from criminal liability on such grounds, as well as to the organizer or leader of an organized group or criminal association.
The Code has been supplemented with an article:
No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, No. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)

Article. Release of a Whistleblower from Criminal Liability

1. A person who has committed a misdemeanor, a negligent crime or a minor or less serious crime may be released from criminal liability by the court if:
1) he has been recognized as a whistleblower under the Law of the Republic of Lithuania on the Protection of Whistleblowers and
2) confessed to having committed a criminal act, and
3) actively assisted in disclosing a criminal act committed by another person, and
4) the criminal act which he assisted in disclosing is, by its nature, more dangerous than the criminal act committed by himself.
2. A dangerous recidivist, a person who has a criminal record, who was the organizer of the criminal act which he assisted in disclosing, may not be released from criminal liability on the grounds specified in paragraph 1 of this Article.
3. If a person released from criminal liability under paragraph 1 of this Article commits a misdemeanor or negligent crime within one year, the court may revoke the decision on release from criminal liability and decide on the criminal liability of such person for all criminal acts committed.
4. If a person released from criminal liability under paragraph 1 of this Article commits a new intentional crime within one year, the previous decision to release from criminal liability shall cease to be valid and the criminal liability of such person for all criminal acts committed shall be decided.
Supplemented with an article:

No. XIII-1844, 2018-12-20, published TAR 2018-12-28,
i. k. 2018-21873

This excerpt sits under: Consolidated version from 2024-01-01

Article. Release from criminal liability on bail

1. A person who has committed a criminal misdemeanour, a negligent or minor or medium gravity intentional crime may be released from criminal liability by the court if there is a request from a person who is worthy of the court's trust to transfer the offender to his responsibility on bail. Bail may be assigned with a deposit or without it.
2. A person may be released from criminal liability on bail by the court if:
1) he committed a criminal act for the first time and
2) he fully admitted his guilt and regrets having committed the criminal act, and
3) he at least partially compensated or eliminated the damage caused or undertook to compensate it, if it was caused, and
4) there is reason to believe that he will fully compensate or eliminate the damage caused, will comply with the laws and will not commit new criminal acts.
3. The guarantor may be the offender's parents, close relatives or other persons worthy of the court's trust. The court, when making a decision, takes into account the guarantor's personal qualities or nature of activity and the possibility of having a positive influence on the offender.
4. The bail term is set from one to three years.
5. When requesting to transfer a person on bail with a deposit, the guarantor undertakes to pay a deposit of the amount determined by the court. Taking into account the guarantor's personal qualities and his financial situation, the court determines the amount of the deposit or decides on the release from criminal liability on bail without a deposit. The deposit is returned upon expiry of the bail term if the person for whom bail was provided did not commit a new criminal act during the bail term set by the court.
6. The guarantor has the right to refuse bail. In this case, the court, taking into account the reasons for refusing bail, decides on the return of the deposit, as well as on the person's criminal liability for the committed criminal act, the appointment of another guarantor or the release of the person from criminal liability.
7. If a person released from criminal liability on bail committed a new criminal misdemeanour or negligent crime during the bail period, the court may revoke the decision to release from criminal liability and decide on this person's criminal liability for all acts committed.
8. If a person released from criminal liability on bail committed a new intentional crime during the bail period, the previous decision to release him from criminal liability ceases to be valid and a decision is made on this person's criminal liability for all criminal acts committed.

VII CHAPTER
PUNISHMENT

Article. Punishment and its purpose

1. Punishment is a measure of state coercion imposed by a court judgment on a person who has committed a crime or criminal misdemeanour.
2. The purpose of punishment:
1) to deter persons from committing criminal acts;
2) to punish a person who has committed a criminal act;
3) to deprive or restrict the convicted person's ability to commit new criminal acts;
4) to influence persons who have served their punishment to comply with the laws and not to commit crimes again;
5) to ensure the implementation of the principle of justice.

Article. Types of punishments

1. The following punishments may be imposed on a person who has committed a crime:
1) community service;
2) fine;
3) restriction of liberty;
4) arrest;
5) fixed-term imprisonment;

6) life imprisonment.
2. The following punishments may be imposed on a person who has committed a criminal misdemeanour:
1) community service;
2) fine;
3) restriction of liberty;
4) arrest.
3. Only one punishment may be imposed on a person who has committed one crime or criminal misdemeanour.
4. In the cases provided for in Articles 63 and 64 of this Code, two punishments may be imposed.
5. If more than two punishments of different types are imposed for several crimes committed, the court, when imposing the final aggregate punishment, leaves two punishments out of the imposed punishments: one of them – the most severe, and the other it selects at its discretion.
6. One or more of the following criminal impact measures may be imposed on a person who has committed a criminal act together with the punishment, based on Articles 67, 68, 681, 682, 71, 72, 721, 722, 723 and 724 of this Code – prohibition to use a special right, deprivation of public rights, deprivation of the right to perform certain work or engage in certain activities, payment to the fund for victims of crimes, confiscation of property, obligation to live separately from the victim and (or) not to approach the victim closer than a specified distance, participation in programmes changing violent behaviour, extended confiscation of property, obligation to report a change of residence or departure from it. Payment to the fund for victims of crimes is not imposed together with a fine punishment.
Article part amendments:
No. XII-1676, 2015-05-07, published TAR 2015-05-18,
i. k. 2015-07563
No. XIII-2263, 2019-06-27, published TAR 2019-07-05,

No. XIII-3353, 2020-11-05, published TAR 2020-11-18,
i. k. 2020-24238

7. The types of punishments for legal persons and the specifics of imposing punishments on minors are established by Articles 43 and 90 of this Code.

Article. Types of punishments for legal persons

1. The following punishments may be imposed on a legal person for a committed criminal act:
1) fine;
2) restriction of the legal person's activities;
3) liquidation of the legal person.
2. The court, having imposed a punishment on a legal person, may decide to publish this judgment through the mass media. A judgment by which the court imposes a punishment on a legal person for crimes provided for in Chapter XXXIII of this Code must be published through the mass media.
Article part amendments:
No. XIII-653, 2017-09-28, published TAR 2017-10-05,
i. k. 2017-15859

3. Only one punishment may be imposed on a legal person for one criminal act.
4. Punishments imposed on legal persons are not specified in the sanctions of the articles of the Special Part of this Code. The court, when imposing a punishment on a legal person, is guided by the list of punishments established in part 1 of this Article.
5. One or more of the following criminal impact measures may be imposed on a legal person together with the punishment, based on Articles 67, 71, 72 and 723 of this Code – payment to the fund for victims of crimes, confiscation of property, extended confiscation of property. Payment to the fund for victims of crimes may be imposed only with the punishment of restriction of the legal person's activities.

Article. Community Service

1.	The court imposes community service in the cases provided for in the Special Part of this Code. Community service is imposed only if the accused consents.
Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-2263, 2019-06-27, published TAR 2019-07-05,
i. k. 2019-11177

2.	Community service is imposed from one month to one year. The term of the sentence is calculated in months. A person sentenced to perform community service is obliged to work unpaid from 10 to 40 hours per month for the benefit of society within the time period set by the court.
3.	The time for performing community service and the number of working hours is not indicated in the sanction of the article. This is determined by the court when imposing the sentence, however, this time may not exceed 480 hours when the sentence is imposed for a crime, and 240 hours when the sentence is imposed for a misdemeanour.
4.	Work for the convicted person is selected by the institution executing the sentence together with the executive institutions of municipalities.
5.	If, due to objective reasons, a person cannot perform the community service imposed on him under this Article, the court may release him from this sentence, instead imposing one or more measures of criminal impact provided for in Chapter IX of this Code or one or more measures of educational impact provided for in Chapter XI, when a minor is released from the sentence of community service. Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-2263, 2019-06-27, published TAR 2019-07-05,
i. k. 2019-11177

6.	If a person, for justifiable reasons, has not worked the prescribed community service hours within the time period set by the court, the court may extend the time for community service until that person works the prescribed hours.
7.	If a person evades performing community service, the institution executing the sentence warns the convicted person of possible legal consequences. If the person continues to evade performing community service after the warning, the court, upon the proposal of the institution executing the sentence, may replace community service with a fine or restriction of liberty. Community service is replaced with a fine or restriction of liberty according to the rules established in Articles 47, 48 and 65 of this Code.
Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-2263, 2019-06-27, published TAR 2019-07-05,
i. k. 2019-11177

8.	Ceased to be valid 2019-07-06. Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-2263, 2019-06-27, published TAR 2019-07-05,

Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, No. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)
No. XI-742, 2010-04-13, Žin., 2010, No. 48-2292 (2010-04-27)

Article. Fine

1.	A fine is a pecuniary penalty imposed by the court in the cases provided for in the Special Part of this Code.
2.	A fine is calculated in minimum subsistence level (MGL) amounts.
3.	The following fines are established:
1)	for a misdemeanour – from 15 to 500 MGL amounts;
2)	for a minor crime – from 50 to 2,000 MGL amounts;

3)	for a less serious crime – from 100 to 4,000 MGL amounts;
4)	for a serious crime – from 150 to 6,000 MGL amounts;
5)	for a negligent crime – from 20 to 750 MGL amounts.
4.	For a legal person, a fine from 200 to 100,000 MGL amounts is established.
5.	The amount of the fine for a criminal act committed is not indicated in the sanction of the article. It is determined by the court when imposing the sentence.
6.	A fine imposed for criminal acts provided for in Chapter XXXIII of this Code may not be less than the established amount of the object of the criminal act, the property damage caused by the offender or the property benefit obtained or sought to be obtained by the offender for himself or another person. Where there are several criteria for calculating the amount of the fine, the amount of the fine imposed is calculated according to the one whose value, expressed in money, is the greatest. When a fine is imposed in accordance with the rules established in this part, the final fine imposed by the court for criminal acts provided for in Chapter XXXIII of this Code may exceed the maximum fine amounts specified in parts 3 and 4 of this Article, but may not be less than the minimum fine amounts provided for in parts 3 and 4 of this Article.
7.	If a person does not have the means to pay the fine imposed by the court, the court, following the rules established in Article 65 of this Code, may, with the consent of the convicted person, replace this sentence with community service.
8.	If a person evades voluntarily paying the fine and there is no possibility to collect it, the court may replace the fine with restriction of liberty. When replacing a fine with restriction of liberty, the court follows the rules established in Articles 48 and 65 of this Code.
Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-2263, 2019-06-27, published TAR 2019-07-05,
i. k. 2019-11177

Amendments to the Article:
No. XIII-653, 2017-09-28, published TAR 2017-10-05,
i. k. 2017-15859

This excerpt sits under: Consolidated version from 2024-01-01

Article. Restriction of Liberty

1.	The court imposes a restriction of liberty sentence in cases provided for in the Special Part of this Code.
2.	A restriction of liberty sentence may be imposed from three months to two years. The term of the sentence is calculated in years and months.
3.	When imposing a restriction of liberty sentence, the court usually assigns intensive supervision to the accused – control of the convicted person's location at a specified time by means of electronic monitoring. The location and time of the convicted person are determined by the court.
4.	Together with intensive supervision or instead of it, the court may oblige a person to whom a restriction of liberty sentence has been imposed to:
1)	participate in a behaviour correction programme;
2)	work without remuneration for up to 200 hours during a period determined by the court, but not longer than the restriction of liberty period, in health care, social service institutions or non-governmental organisations that care for elderly persons, persons with disabilities or other persons in need of assistance;
3)	compensate for the property damage caused by the criminal act or part thereof, or remedy such damage by their own work;
4)	start working or register with the Employment Service under the Ministry of Social Security and Labour of the Republic of Lithuania, study;
5)	undergo treatment for alcoholism, drug addiction, substance abuse or venereal disease, when the convicted person consents.
5.	Together with intensive supervision, obligations or instead of them, the court may prohibit a person to whom a restriction of liberty sentence has been imposed from:
1)	visiting certain places;
2)	communicating with certain persons or groups of persons;
3)	possessing, using, acquiring, storing personally or transferring for storage to other persons certain objects;
4)	using psychoactive substances.
6.	The court may, at the request of other participants in criminal proceedings, as well as at its own discretion, impose on a person to whom a restriction of liberty sentence has been imposed one or more measures of criminal impact provided for in Chapter IX of this Code or one or more measures of educational impact provided for in Chapter XI, when a restriction of liberty sentence is imposed on a minor, and (or) other prohibitions or obligations not provided for in the criminal law, if, in the court's opinion, this would have a positive effect on their behaviour.
7.	During the execution of a restriction of liberty sentence, the court may, upon the proposal of the institution executing the sentence, replace intensive supervision, imposed measures of criminal impact, measures of educational impact, obligations and (or) prohibitions with other measures of criminal impact provided for in Chapter IX of this Code or measures of educational impact provided for in Chapter XI, when a restriction of liberty sentence has been imposed on a minor, obligations, prohibitions, impose additional measures of criminal impact provided for in Chapter IX of this Code or measures of educational impact provided for in Chapter XI, when a restriction of liberty sentence has been imposed on a minor, and (or) obligations and (or) prohibitions referred to in paragraphs 4, 5 and 6 of this Article, as well as intensive supervision, if this would have a positive effect on the convicted person's behaviour.
8.	If the convicted person evades serving the restriction of liberty sentence, the court may, upon the proposal of the institution executing the sentence, additionally impose measures of criminal impact provided for in Chapter IX of this Code or measures of educational impact provided for in Chapter XI, when a restriction of liberty sentence has been imposed on a minor, and (or) obligations and (or) prohibitions referred to in paragraphs 4, 5 and 6 of this Article, as well as intensive supervision, or replace the restriction of liberty sentence with arrest. A restriction of liberty sentence is replaced with arrest according to the rules established in Articles 49 and 65 of this Code.
Amendments to the Article:

Article. Arrest

1.	The court imposes arrest in cases provided for in the Special Part of this Code.
2.	Arrest is short-term deprivation of liberty. The term of arrest is calculated in twenty-four-hour periods.
Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-2263, 2019-06-27, published in TAR 2019-07-05,
i. k. 2019-11177

3.	For a crime, arrest is established from fifteen to ninety twenty-four-hour periods, for a criminal misdemeanour – from ten to forty-five twenty-four-hour periods.
4.	The term of arrest for a criminal act is not indicated in the sanction of the article. It is determined by the court when imposing the sentence.
5.	Expired 2019-07-06. Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-2263, 2019-06-27, published in TAR 2019-07-05,
i. k. 2019-11177

6.	Arrest is not imposed on pregnant women and may not be imposed on persons raising a child under three years of age, taking into account the interests of the child.

Article. Fixed-term Deprivation of Liberty

1.	The court imposes fixed-term deprivation of liberty in cases provided for in the Special Part of this Code. The term of the sentence is calculated in years, months and days.
2.	A fixed-term deprivation of liberty sentence may be imposed from three months to twenty years. If a sentence is imposed under Article 64 of this Code, when a new crime is committed without having served the sentence, a deprivation of liberty sentence of up to twenty-five years may be imposed.
3.	Expired 2020-07-01. Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-2263, 2019-06-27, published in TAR 2019-07-05,
i. k. 2019-11177

Article. Life Imprisonment

1.	The court imposes a life imprisonment sentence in cases provided for in the Special Part of this Code.

2.	After the convicted person has served twenty years of a life imprisonment sentence, the question of replacing the life imprisonment sentence imposed on them with a fixed-term deprivation of liberty sentence is examined and resolved by the court upon the proposal of the institution executing the sentence. If the court decides to replace the life imprisonment sentence with a fixed-term deprivation of liberty sentence, the term of the fixed-term deprivation of liberty sentence may not be shorter than five years and longer than ten years from the date of entry into force of the court's ruling. When deciding the question of replacing a life imprisonment sentence with a fixed-term deprivation of liberty sentence, the court takes into account the convicted person's risk of criminal behaviour, the convicted person's behaviour during the serving of the sentence, the objectives of the sentence and the effect of the served sentence on the convicted person, whether the convicted person has compensated for or remedied a significant part of the damage caused and undertakes to fully compensate for or remedy it. The procedure for replacing a life imprisonment sentence with a fixed-term deprivation of liberty sentence is established by the Code of Criminal Procedure of the Republic of Lithuania.
Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-2005, 2019-03-21, published in TAR 2019-04-02,
i. k. 2019-05331

3.	Expired from 2023-01-01 Deletion of the part of the Article:
No. XIV-1199, 2022-06-28, published in TAR 2022-07-15,
i. k. 2022-15557
Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-2263, 2019-06-27, published in TAR 2019-07-05,
i. k. 2019-11177

4.	The twenty-year term of life imprisonment referred to in paragraph 2 of this Article is calculated from the application of detention on remand (arrest) or the commencement of serving the imposed life imprisonment sentence. If the convicted person commits a new intentional crime during the serving of the life imprisonment sentence, the calculation of the twenty-year term referred to in paragraph 2 of this Article is interrupted and the term begins to be calculated anew from the date of commission of the intentional crime.

No. XIII-2005, 2019-03-21, published in TAR 2019-04-02,
