Article. Restriction of a Legal Person's Activities

1. When imposing a penalty of restriction of a legal person's activities, the court prohibits the legal person from engaging in certain activities or obliges it to close a certain division of the legal person.
2. A legal person's activities may be restricted from one to five years. The term of this penalty is calculated in years and months.

Article. Liquidation of a Legal Person

When imposing a penalty of liquidation of a legal person, the court obliges the legal person to cease all economic, commercial, financial or professional activities within a time limit set by the court and to close all divisions of the legal person.

VIII CHAPTER
IMPOSITION OF PENALTIES

Article. General Grounds for Imposition of Penalties

1. The court imposes a penalty according to the sanction of the article of the Special Part of this Code providing for liability for the criminal act committed, observing the provisions of the General Part of this Code.
2. When imposing a penalty, the court takes into account:
1) the degree of danger of the criminal act committed;
2) the form and type of guilt;
3) the motives and purposes of the criminal act committed;
4) the stage of the criminal act;

5) the personality of the offender;
6) the form and type of participation of the person as an accomplice in committing the criminal act;
7) circumstances mitigating and aggravating liability;
8) the harm caused by the criminal act.
Supplemented by an article paragraph:
No. XIII-653, 2017-09-28, published TAR 2017-10-05,
i. k. 2017-15859

3. If the imposition of a penalty provided for in the sanction of an article would clearly contradict the principle of justice, the court, guided by the purpose of the penalty, may impose a more lenient penalty with reasoning.

Article

Imposition of a Penalty on a Person Convicted for the First Time of a Negligent or Minor or Less Serious Intentional Crime
For a person convicted for the first time of a negligent or minor or less serious intentional crime, the court usually imposes penalties not related to arrest or fixed-term deprivation of liberty. When imposing a penalty of arrest or fixed-term deprivation of liberty, the court must give reasons for its decision.
Article amendments:
No. XII-1554, 2015-03-19, published TAR 2015-03-23,
i. k. 2015-04087
No. XIII-2263, 2019-06-27, published TAR 2019-07-05,
i. k. 2019-11177

Article. Imposition of a Penalty on a Dangerous Recidivist for Committing an Intentional Crime

A dangerous recidivist for an intentional crime is imposed a penalty more severe than the average deprivation of liberty penalty established in the sanction of the article for the crime committed. A different penalty may be imposed on a dangerous recidivist only on the grounds provided for in Article 62 of this Code.
Article amendments:
No. XII-1554, 2015-03-19, published TAR 2015-03-23,
i. k. 2015-04087

Article

Imposition of a Penalty for Preparation and Attempt to Commit a Criminal Act
1. A penalty for preparation or attempt to commit a crime or criminal misdemeanour is imposed in the general procedure, taking into account the danger of the actions committed by the offender, the extent to which the criminal intent was realised, and the reasons why the criminal act was not completed.
2. For preparation or attempt to commit a criminal act, based on Article 62 of this Code, a more lenient penalty than that provided for a completed criminal act may be imposed.

Article. Imposition of a Penalty on Accomplices to a Criminal Act

1. A penalty for accomplices to a crime or criminal misdemeanour is imposed in the general procedure, taking into account the type, form, role and nature of the person's participation as an accomplice in committing the criminal act.
2. Members of an organised group for committing a crime are usually imposed a more severe penalty than members of a group of accomplices.

Article. Circumstances Mitigating Liability

1. Circumstances mitigating liability are the following:
1) the offender provided assistance to the victim or by other active actions avoided or attempted to avoid more serious consequences;
2) the offender confessed to committing the act provided for by criminal law and sincerely repents or helped to clarify this act or the persons involved in it;
3) the offender voluntarily compensated or eliminated the harm caused;
4) the criminal act was committed due to a very difficult financial or desperate situation of the offender;
5) the act was committed due to mental or physical coercion, if such coercion does not eliminate criminal liability;
6) the commission of the act was influenced by provocative or risky behaviour of the victim;
7) the act was committed at the request of the victim whose condition is hopeless;

8) the act was committed in violation of the conditions of lawfulness of detention of a person who committed a criminal act, extreme necessity, professional duty or performance of a law enforcement task, industrial or economic risk, scientific experiment;
9) the act was committed exceeding the limits of necessary defence, where criminal law provides for liability for exceeding the limits of necessary defence;
10) the act was committed due to great agitation caused by unlawful actions of the victim;
11) the act was committed by a person of limited sanity;
12) the act was committed by a person intoxicated or drugged against his will;
13) unsuccessful voluntary abandonment of committing a criminal act.
2. The court may recognise as circumstances mitigating liability other circumstances not specified in paragraph
1 of this Article.
3. When imposing a penalty, the court does not take into account such a circumstance mitigating liability which is provided for in law as an element of the composition of a crime.

Article. Aggravating circumstances

1. Aggravating circumstances are the following:
1) the act was committed by a group of accomplices. The court, taking into account the nature and degree of each accomplice's participation in committing the criminal act, may not recognise this circumstance as aggravating;
2) the act was committed by an organised group;
3) the act was committed out of hooligan or selfish motives;
4) the act was committed by torturing the victim or otherwise treating them with extreme cruelty or mocking them;
Article point amendments:
No. XIII-3350, 2020-11-05, published TAR 2020-11-18,
i. k. 2020-24234

5) the act was committed against a minor;
6) the act was committed against a person who, due to illness, disability, old age or other reasons, was in a helpless state, without their request, or the act was committed against a minor by exploiting their dependence or by abusing trust, authority or influence;
7) the act was committed against a pregnant woman, when it is obvious that she is pregnant;
8) the act was committed by exploiting a public or another person's misfortune;
9) the act was committed by a person intoxicated by alcohol, narcotic, psychotropic or other psychoactive substances, if these circumstances influenced the commission of the criminal act;
10) the act was committed in a generally dangerous manner or using explosives, explosive substances or firearms;
11) the act committed resulted in serious consequences or created a real threat to the victim's life;
12) the act was committed with the aim of expressing hatred towards a group of persons or a person belonging to it on the grounds of age, sex, sexual orientation, disability, race, skin colour, nationality, language, origin, ethnic origin, social status, religion, beliefs or views;
Article point amendments:
No. XIV-1065, 2022-04-28, published TAR 2022-05-04,
i. k. 2022-09434

13) the act was committed by a recidivist;
Supplemented by article point:
No. XII-1554, 2015-03-19, published TAR 2015-03-23,
i. k. 2015-04087

14) the acts provided for in Article 149 paragraphs 3 and 4, Article 150 paragraphs 3 and 4, Article 151 paragraph 2, Articles 1511, 1521, 153, 162, Article 307 paragraph 3, Article 308 paragraph 3, Article 309 paragraphs 2 and 3 were committed by a close relative, family member or cohabiting person of the victim minor.
Supplemented by article point:
No. XIII-3350, 2020-11-05, published TAR 2020-11-18,
i. k. 2020-24234

2. When imposing a sentence, the court shall not take into account such an aggravating circumstance which is provided for in the law as an element of the crime.
Article amendments:
No. X-272, 2005-06-23, Žin., 2005, No. 81-2945 (2005-06-30)
No. XI-303, 2009-06-16, Žin., 2009, No. 77-3168 (2009-06-30)
No. XII-776, 2014-03-13, published TAR 2014-03-24,
i. k. 2014-03404

Article

Imposition of a sentence when there are mitigating and (or) aggravating circumstances
1. When imposing a sentence, the court shall take into account whether only mitigating circumstances or only aggravating circumstances have been established, or whether there are both mitigating and aggravating circumstances, and shall assess the significance of each circumstance.
2. The court, having assessed the mitigating and (or) aggravating circumstances, their number, nature and interrelation, as well as other circumstances specified in Article 54 paragraph 2, shall reasonably select a milder or more severe type of sentence, as well as the amount of the sentence to be imposed, calculating from its average.
3. When determining the average of the sentence provided for by law, the minimum and maximum amount of the sentence provided for in the sanction of the article shall be added and the result obtained shall be divided in half. If the minimum amount of the sentence for the criminal act committed is not specified in the sanction of the article, when determining the average sentence, the minimum amount of that type of sentence shall be used.
4. If the offender voluntarily confessed to having committed the crime, sincerely repents, actively helped to clarify the crime and there are no aggravating circumstances, the court shall impose on them a custodial sentence not exceeding the average of the sentence provided for in the sanction of the article for the crime committed, or a non-custodial sentence.
5. A custodial sentence not exceeding the average of the sentence provided for in the sanction of the article for the crime committed may be imposed by the court on a person who participated in intentional killing, if they confessed to all their criminal acts and actively helped to disclose an intentional killing committed by members of an organised group or criminal association.
6. Paragraph 5 of this Article shall not apply to the organiser or leader of an intentional killing, organised group or criminal association.
Article amendments:
No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, Nr. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)

Article. Imposition of a milder sentence than provided for by law

1. The court, having regard to all the circumstances of the case, may impose a milder sentence than provided for by law for each criminal act, if the person who committed the criminal act voluntarily came or reported this act, confessed to having committed it and sincerely repents, and (or) helped the pre-trial investigation and the court to clarify the criminal act, and fully or partially compensated or eliminated the property damage caused.
2. The court, having regard to all the circumstances of the case, may impose a milder sentence than provided for by law for each criminal act also in cases where there are mitigating circumstances, the property damage has been at least partially compensated or eliminated, if it was caused, and:
1) the offender supports persons who suffer from a serious illness or are disabled and there is no one to care for them, or
2) the offender supports minor children who, due to the sentence provided for by law, would have no one to care for them, or
3) the offender's role as an accomplice in committing the criminal act was secondary, or
4) the act was interrupted during preparation to commit a crime or attempt to commit a criminal act, or
5) the act was committed by exceeding the limits of necessary defence, or
6) the act was committed in violation of the conditions of lawfulness of apprehension of a person who committed a criminal act, extreme necessity, professional duty or performance of a law enforcement agency's task, industrial or economic risk, scientific experiment.
3. Where the conditions specified in paragraphs 1 and 2 of this Article exist, the court may:
1) impose a lesser sentence than the minimum sentence provided for in the sanction of the article for the criminal act committed, or

2) impose a lesser sentence than provided for in Article 47 paragraphs 3, 4 and 6, Article 56 and Article 90 paragraph 3 of this Code, or
Article point amendments:
No. XII-1554, 2015-03-19, published TAR 2015-03-23,
i. k. 2015-04087
No. XIII-653, 2017-09-28, published TAR 2017-10-05,
i. k. 2017-15859

3) impose a milder type of sentence than provided for in the sanction of the article for the criminal act committed.
4. The court may also, pursuant to paragraph 3 of this Article, impose a milder sentence than provided for by law on a person who participated in intentional killing, if they confessed to all their criminal acts and actively helped to disclose an intentional killing committed by members of an organised group or criminal association, and:
1) the killing was committed due to threat or coercion, or
2) the offender's role as an accomplice in the killing was secondary, or
3) the act was interrupted during preparation to kill or attempt to kill.
Article amendments:
No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, Nr. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)

Article. Imposition of Punishment for Multiple Criminal Acts

1.	If multiple criminal acts have been committed, the court shall impose a punishment for each criminal act separately, and then impose a final aggregate punishment. When imposing the final aggregate punishment, the court may absorb the punishments or add them together in full or in part.
2.	When punishments are absorbed, the more severe punishment absorbs the less severe ones and the final aggregate punishment equals the most severe of the punishments imposed for the individual criminal acts.
3.	When punishments are added together in full, all the less severe punishments imposed are added to the most severe punishment imposed for one of the criminal acts committed.
4.	When punishments are added together in part, the less severe punishments are added in part to the most severe punishment imposed for one of the criminal acts committed, and the reasons for selecting each of the parts of the punishments to be added are indicated. The part of the punishment to be added may not be less than the minimum amount of that type of punishment, except in cases where the part of the unserved punishment to be added is less than that amount.
Article section amendments:
No. XIII-653, 2017-09-28, published TAR 2017-10-05,
i. k. 2017-15859

5.	The court shall apply absorption of punishments when:
1)	there is an ideal concurrence of criminal acts;
2)	the criminal acts committed differ greatly in terms of dangerousness and are classified as different types or categories of criminal acts under Articles 10 or 11 of this Code;
3)	a punishment of twenty years' imprisonment or life imprisonment has been imposed for one criminal act.
6.	If, when imposing the final punishment, some of the imposed punishments may be absorbed and others may only be added together in full or in part, the court shall aggregate the punishments by way of absorption and addition of punishments. The court shall choose the procedure for aggregating punishments having assessed the nature and dangerousness of the criminal acts committed.
7.	When a punishment is imposed in accordance with paragraph 1 of this Article, the final aggregate punishment may not exceed twenty years' imprisonment, or, if another type of punishment is imposed, except a fine, the maximum amount of that type of punishment established by this Code.
Article section amendments:
No. XIII-653, 2017-09-28, published TAR 2017-10-05,
i. k. 2017-15859

8.	It is not possible to impose a type of aggregate punishment that was not imposed for the individual criminal acts.
9.	A punishment shall be imposed in accordance with the rules of this Article also in cases where, after the adoption of a judgment, it is established that the person committed another crime or criminal misdemeanour before the adoption of the judgment in the first case. In this case, the punishment served in full or in part under the earlier judgment shall be credited towards the term of punishment.

10.	A person shall not be deemed to have committed multiple criminal acts if he or she has committed a continuing criminal act.
Article amendments:
No. IX-2314, 2004-07-05, Žin., 2004, No. 108-4030 (2004-07-13)
No. X-1233, 2007-06-28, Žin., 2007, No. 81-3309 (2007-07-21)

Article

Imposition of Punishment Where a New Criminal Act is Committed Without Having Served the Punishment
1.	If a convicted person commits a new criminal act without having served the imposed punishment, or if a person whose execution of punishment has been suspended commits a new criminal act during the period of suspension of execution of punishment, the court, having imposed a punishment for the new crime or criminal misdemeanour, shall aggregate the punishments. When imposing an aggregate punishment, the court may add the punishments together in full or in part.
2.	When punishments are added together in full, the entire unserved part of the punishment is added to the punishment imposed by the new judgment.
3.	When punishments are added together in part, a part of the unserved punishment is added to the punishment imposed by the new judgment. If the unserved part of the punishment is greater, then a part of the punishment imposed by the new judgment is added to it. The part of the punishment to be added may not be less than the minimum amount of that type of punishment, except in cases where the part of the unserved punishment to be added is less than that amount.
Article section amendments:
No. XIII-653, 2017-09-28, published TAR 2017-10-05,
i. k. 2017-15859

4.	When a punishment is imposed in accordance with paragraph 1 of this Article, the aggregate punishment may not exceed twenty-five years' imprisonment, or, if another type of punishment is imposed, except a fine, the maximum amount of that type of punishment established by this Code.
Article section amendments:
No. XIII-653, 2017-09-28, published TAR 2017-10-05,
i. k. 2017-15859

5.	If a punishment of life imprisonment was imposed for one of the crimes committed, the punishments shall be aggregated by way of absorption and the aggregate punishment shall be life imprisonment.
Article amendments:
No. XI-1861, 2011-12-22, Žin., 2012, No. 5-138 (2012-01-07)

Article

Imposition of Punishment Where a Case is Heard by Simplified Procedure or Abbreviated Examination of Evidence is Conducted
1.	Where a criminal case is heard by expedited procedure or abbreviated examination of evidence is conducted, and also where a criminal case is concluded by a criminal order of the court, the convicted person shall be imposed a punishment which shall be reduced by one third by the same judgment.
2.	The rule established in paragraph 1 of this Article shall apply only in cases where the person pleads guilty.
Code supplemented by article:
No. XII-499, 2013-07-02, Žin., 2013, No. 75-3770 (2013-07-13)

Article. Rules for Addition and Conversion of Punishments

1.	Imposed punishments shall be added together, and also one punishment shall be converted into another punishment, according to the following rules:
1)	one day of imprisonment equals:
a)	one day of arrest (1:1);
b)	two days of restriction of liberty (1:2);
2)	one day of arrest equals:
a)	a fine of two MSL amounts (1:2);
b)	six hours of community service (1:6);
c)	two days of restriction of liberty (1:2);
3)	one day of restriction of liberty equals:
a)	three hours of community service (1:3);

b)	a fine of one MSL amount (1:1);
4)	a fine of one MSL amount equals six hours of community service.
2.	The court, when aggregating punishments in the cases provided for in Articles 63 and 64 of this Code, shall convert the less severe punishment into a more severe punishment. A fine shall not be converted and shall be imposed together with another punishment. Double conversion of punishments is prohibited.
Article amendments:
No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, No. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)

Article. Crediting of Detention on Remand Towards the Imposed Punishment

1.	The court, when imposing a punishment on a person to whom detention on remand (arrest) was applied, must credit it towards the imposed punishment.
2.	Detention on remand (arrest) shall be credited towards the imposed punishment according to the rules of paragraph 1 of Article 65 of this Code, equating one day of detention on remand (arrest) to one day of imprisonment or arrest, a fine of two MSL amounts, six hours of community service, two days of restriction of liberty.
Article amendments:
No. X-1233, 2007-06-28, Žin., 2007, No. 81-3309 (2007-07-21)
CHAPTER IX
CRIMINAL IMPACT MEASURES AND THEIR IMPOSITION

Article. Purpose and types of penal measures

1.	Penal measures shall help to implement the purpose of punishment.
2.	An adult person released from criminal liability on the grounds provided for in Chapter VI of this Code or released from punishment on the grounds provided for in Chapter X of this Code, or conditionally released from a place of deprivation of liberty, may be assigned the following penal measures:
Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIV-1199, 2022-06-28, published TAR 2022-07-15,
i. k. 2022-15557
1)	prohibition on exercising a special right;
2)	deprivation of public rights;
3)	deprivation of the right to perform certain work or engage in certain activities;
4)	compensation or elimination of property damage;
5)	unpaid work;
6)	contribution to the fund for victims of crime;
7)	confiscation of property;
8)	obligation to live separately from the victim and (or) not to approach the victim closer than a specified distance;
9)	participation in programmes changing violent behaviour;
10)	extended confiscation of property;
11)	obligation to report a change of place of residence or departure from it.
Supplemented by an Article point:
No. XIII-3353, 2020-11-05, published TAR 2020-11-18,
i. k. 2020-24238

3.	Prohibition on exercising a special right, deprivation of public rights, deprivation of the right to perform certain work or engage in certain activities, contribution to the fund for victims of crime, confiscation of property, obligation to live separately from the victim and (or) not to approach the victim closer than a specified distance, participation in programmes changing violent behaviour, extended confiscation of property, obligation to report a change of place of residence or departure from it may be assigned together with punishment. A contribution to the fund for victims of crime shall not be assigned together with a fine.
Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-3353, 2020-11-05, published TAR 2020-11-18,
i. k. 2020-24238

4.	A minor released from criminal liability on the grounds provided for in Chapter VI or XI of this Code or released from punishment on the grounds provided for in Chapter X of this Code or in Article 92 may be assigned a contribution to the fund for victims of crime, confiscation of property, extended confiscation of property. A contribution to the fund for victims of crime shall not be assigned together with a fine.
5.	A legal person may be assigned a contribution to the fund for victims of crime, confiscation of property, extended confiscation of property. A contribution to the fund for victims of crime shall not be assigned together with a fine.
6.	Where penal measures are assigned together with punishment or two or more penal measures are assigned, account shall be taken of their compatibility and possibilities to have a corrective effect on the convicted person.

Article. Prohibition on exercising a special right

1.	The court may prohibit a person from exercising special rights (the right to drive road, air or water vehicles, the right to possess and carry weapons, the right to hunt, the right to fish, etc.) in cases where the person committed a criminal act while exercising these rights.
2.	The court shall prohibit the exercise of special rights for a period from one to five years. For criminal acts provided for in paragraphs 2, 4, 6 of Article 281 or in Article 2811 of this Code, the right to drive road vehicles may be prohibited for a period from one to seven years for a person who, at the time of committing these criminal acts, was already prohibited from exercising the right to drive road vehicles in the manner established by this Code due to a criminal act committed by him while intoxicated or under the influence of narcotic, psychotropic or other psychoactive substances. The periods specified in this paragraph shall be calculated in years, months and days.
Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-1929, 2019-01-11, published TAR 2019-01-21,
i. k. 2019-00859
No. XIV-1447, 2022-10-10, published TAR 2022-10-19,
i. k. 2022-21163

3.	The court, when prohibiting a person from exercising special rights, shall specifically indicate which right or rights may not be exercised and the term of this prohibition.
4.	A prohibition on exercising a special right assigned together with deprivation of liberty or arrest shall apply for the entire period of serving the deprivation of liberty or arrest and for the period assigned by the court after serving the deprivation of liberty or arrest.

Article

Deprivation of public rights or deprivation of the right to perform certain work or engage in certain activities
1.	Deprivation of public rights is the deprivation of the right to be elected or the right to be appointed to elected or appointed positions in state or municipal institutions and establishments, enterprises or non-governmental organisations. A person may be assigned the deprivation of one or both of these rights.
2.	The court shall assign deprivation of public rights in cases where a criminal act was committed by abusing public rights.
3.	The court shall assign deprivation of the right to perform certain work or engage in certain activities in cases where a person commits a criminal act in the field of employment or professional activity or where the court, taking into account the nature of the criminal act committed, concludes that the person cannot be allowed to retain the right to perform certain work or engage in certain activities.
4.	Public rights may be deprived or the right to perform certain work or engage in certain activities may be deprived for a period from one to five years. For medium-gravity and serious crimes provided for in Chapter XXXIII of this Code, public rights may be deprived or the right to perform certain work or engage in certain activities may be deprived for a period from three to seven years. The court, when assigning deprivation of public rights or deprivation of the right to perform certain work or engage in certain activities, shall indicate which of the rights specified in paragraphs 1 and 3 of this Article are deprived, and shall also indicate the term of this penal measure. This term shall be calculated in years, months and days.
5.	Deprivation of public rights or deprivation of the right to perform certain work or engage in certain activities assigned together with deprivation of liberty or arrest shall apply for the entire period of serving the deprivation of liberty or arrest and for the period assigned by the court after serving the deprivation of liberty or arrest.

Article. Compensation or elimination of property damage

1.	The court shall assign compensation or elimination of property damage where damage was caused to a person, property or nature as a result of a crime or criminal misdemeanour.
2.	The amounts received by the victim from insurance or other institutions to cover the damage suffered shall not be included in the amount of damage to be compensated.
3.	The damage must be compensated or eliminated within the period set by the court.

Article. Unpaid work

1. The court shall assign from 20 to 100 hours of unpaid work in health care, social services or other state or non-state institutions and organisations. When assigning unpaid work, the court shall set a time limit within which it must be performed. This time limit may not exceed one year.
2. Unpaid work shall be assigned only if the person consents.

Article. Payment into the fund for victims of crime

1. The court may impose on a person a payment into the fund for victims of crime from 10 to 250 MGL, and on a minor – from 3 to 25 MGL. A payment into the fund for victims of crime shall be imposed on a minor only if the minor is working or has property of his or her own. Article part amendments:
No. XIV-368, 2021-05-27, published TAR 2021-06-09,
i. k. 2021-13176

2. The court may impose on a legal person a payment into the fund for victims of crime from 100 to 2 000 MGL.
3. When imposing a payment into the fund for victims of crime, the court shall at the same time set a time limit for payment of this amount. This time limit may not exceed three years.

Article. Confiscation of property

1. Confiscation of property is the compulsory uncompensated seizure into state ownership of property subject to confiscation of any form held by the offender or other persons.
2. Property subject to confiscation shall be deemed to be an instrument, means or proceeds of a prohibited act under this Code. The proceeds of a prohibited act under this Code shall be deemed to be property of any form obtained directly or indirectly from it.
3. Property subject to confiscation belonging to the offender must be confiscated in all cases.

4. Property subject to confiscation belonging to another natural or legal person shall be confiscated, regardless of whether that person has been convicted of committing a prohibited act under this Code or not, if:
1) when transferring the property to the offender or other persons, he or she knew or should and could have known that this property would be used to commit a prohibited act under this Code;
2) this property was transferred to him or her under a sham transaction;
3) this property was transferred to him or her as a family member or close relative of the offender;
4) this property was transferred to him or her as a legal person whose director, member of a management body or participants holding not less than fifty per cent of the shares (stakes, contributions, etc.) of the legal person are the offender, his or her family members or close relatives;
5) when acquiring this property, he or she or persons who held managerial positions in the legal person and had the right to represent it, to make decisions on behalf of the legal person or to control the activities of the legal person knew or should and could have known that this property is an instrument, means or proceeds of a prohibited act under this Code.
5. Where property subject to confiscation is concealed, consumed, belongs to third parties or cannot be seized for other reasons, or where it would be inappropriate to confiscate such property, the court shall recover from the offender or other persons referred to in paragraph 4 of this Article a sum of money corresponding to the value of the property subject to confiscation.
6. When imposing confiscation of property, the court must specify the items to be confiscated or the value of the property to be confiscated in money.
Article amendments:
No. IX-2314, 2004-07-05, Žin., 2004, No. 108-4030 (2004-07-13)
No. XI-1199, 2010-12-02, Žin., 2010, No. 145-7439 (2010-12-11)

Article

Obligation to live separately from the victim and (or) not to approach the victim closer than a specified distance
1. The court may impose an obligation to live separately from the victim and (or) not to approach the victim closer than a specified distance if this is necessary to protect the legitimate interests of the victim.
2. Upon imposition of an obligation to live separately from the victim and (or) not to approach the victim closer than a specified distance, until the end of the time limit set by the court, the offender shall be prohibited from communicating and seeking contact with the victim in any form, from visiting specified places where the victim is present.

Article. Participation in programmes for changing violent behaviour

The court shall oblige persons who have committed criminal acts against a close relative or family member to participate in programmes for changing violent behaviour. This obligation must be fulfilled within the time limit set by the court.
The Code has been supplemented with an article:
No. X-1597, 2008-06-12, Žin., 2008, No. 73-2796 (2008-06-27)

Article. Extended confiscation of property

1. Extended confiscation of property is the seizure into state ownership of the offender's property or part thereof disproportionate to the offender's lawful income, where there is reason to believe that the property was obtained by criminal means.
2. Extended confiscation of property shall be applied where all of the following conditions are met:
1) the offender has been found to have committed a medium gravity, serious or very serious intentional crime from which he or she had or could have had pecuniary benefit;
2) the offender has property acquired at the time of committing the prohibited act under this Code, after its commission or within five years before its commission, the value of which does not correspond to his or her lawful income, and this difference exceeds the amount of 250 MGL, or has transferred such property to other persons during the period specified in this point;
3) during the criminal proceedings, the offender does not substantiate the lawfulness of the acquisition of this property.

3. Property subject to confiscation referred to in paragraph 2 of this Article, transferred to another natural or legal person, shall be confiscated from that person if there is at least one of the following grounds:
1) the property was transferred under a sham transaction;
2) the property was transferred to family members or close relatives of the offender;
3) the property was transferred to a legal person whose director, member of a management body or participants holding not less than fifty per cent of the shares (stakes, contributions, etc.) of the legal person are the offender, his or her family members or close relatives;
4) the person to whom the property was transferred, or persons who held managerial positions in the legal person and had the right to represent it, to make decisions on behalf of the legal person or to control the activities of the legal person knew or should and could have known that this property was acquired by criminal means or with unlawful funds of the offender.
4. The extended confiscation of property provided for in this Article may not be applied to the property or part thereof of the offender or third parties from which recovery cannot be made under international treaties of the Republic of Lithuania, provisions of the Code of Civil Procedure of the Republic of Lithuania, or other laws.
5. Where all the property or part thereof subject to confiscation is concealed, consumed, belongs to third parties or cannot be seized for other reasons, or where it would be inappropriate to confiscate such property, the court shall recover from the offender or other persons referred to in paragraph 3 of this Article a sum of money corresponding to the value of the property or part thereof subject to confiscation.
6. When imposing extended confiscation of property, the court must specify the items to be confiscated or the value of the property or part thereof to be confiscated in money.
The Code has been supplemented with an article:
No. XI-1199, 2010-12-02, Žin., 2010, No. 145-7439 (2010-12-11)

Article

Obligation to report change of residence or departure from it
1. The court imposes the obligation to report change of residence or departure from it in cases where a person has committed criminal acts provided for in parts 3 and 4 of Article 149, parts 3 and 4 of Article 150, part 2 of Article 151, Articles 153, 157, part 1 of Article 162, part 3 of Article 307, part 3 of Article 308 and parts 2 and 3 of Article 309 of this Code.
2. The obligation to report change of residence or departure from it may be imposed from one to five years. When imposing the obligation to report change of residence or departure from it, the court specifies the term of this criminal impact measure. This term is calculated in years and months.
3. The obligation to report change of residence or departure from it, imposed together with fixed-term imprisonment or arrest, applies for the entire period of serving the imprisonment sentence or arrest sentence and the period specified by the court after serving the imprisonment sentence or arrest sentence, and in case of conditional release from a place of deprivation of liberty or postponement of execution of the sentence – during the probation period and the remaining period after the end of probation.

Upon imposition of the obligation to report change of residence or departure from it, until the end of the term set by the court, a person planning to change residence or depart from the place of residence for longer than seven days must report this to the police institution in whose territory the person's place of residence is located before its change, no later than within three days from departure.

Article. Imposition of criminal impact measures

1. The court imposes criminal impact measures in accordance with the provisions of this chapter.
2. Criminal impact measures are not combined with sentences and are executed separately.

Article. Legal consequences of non-execution of criminal impact measures

1. A criminal impact measure provided for in point 4 or 5 of part 2 of Article 67 of this Code imposed by the court, which a person cannot execute due to justifiable reasons, may be replaced by another criminal impact measure at his or her request.
2. A person who evades execution of a criminal impact measure imposed on him or her (except confiscation of property) is liable under Article 243 of this Code.

X CHAPTER
POSTPONEMENT OF EXECUTION OF SENTENCE AND RELEASE FROM SENTENCE

Article. Postponement of execution of sentence

1. For a person sentenced to arrest for the first time, the court may postpone the execution of the imposed sentence from three months to one year and impose intensive supervision on him or her – control of the convicted person's location according to the established time by electronic monitoring means. The location and time of the convicted person are determined by the court. Together with intensive supervision, the court may impose on the convicted person one or several mutually coordinated criminal impact measures provided for in Chapter IX of this Code and (or) the duties established in parts 5 and 6 of this Article.
2. For a person sentenced to imprisonment for no more than six years for crimes committed through negligence or for no more than four years for one or several minor or less serious intentional crimes, the court may postpone the execution of the imposed sentence from one to three years.
3. For a person sentenced to imprisonment for more than six years for crimes committed through negligence or for more than four years for one or several minor or less serious intentional crimes, or for no more than five years for a serious crime, except for serious crimes provided for in Chapters XV, XVI, XVIII, XX, XXI, XXIII, XXXIII, XXXV, XXXVII, XLIV of this Code, the court may partially postpone the execution of the imposed sentence. When partially postponing the execution of the sentence, the court determines the part of the imprisonment sentence to be served immediately after the entry into force of the court judgment, which may not be shorter than six months and longer than two years, as well as the term of postponement of execution of the remaining unserved sentence from one to three years.
4. The execution of the sentence under parts 1, 2, 3 of this Article may be postponed if the court decides that there is sufficient ground to believe that the purposes of the sentence will be achieved without actual serving of the sentence or after the person has served only part of it.
5. When postponing the execution of the sentence under parts 2 and 3 of this Article, the court imposes on the convicted person one or several mutually coordinated criminal impact measures provided for in Chapter IX of this Code, the intensive supervision established in part 1 of this Article and (or) the following duties:
1) to apologize to the victim;
2) to provide assistance to the victim while he or she is receiving treatment;
3) to receive treatment for addiction diseases when the convicted person consents;
4) to raise and supervise his or her minor children, take care of their health, support them;
5) to start working or studying, continue work or studies;
6) to participate in a behavior correction program;
7) not to leave home at a certain time if it is not related to work or studies;
8) not to leave the city (district) of residence without permission of the institution supervising the convicted person;
9) not to visit certain places or not to communicate with certain persons or groups of persons;
10) not to use psychoactive substances;
11) not to possess, use, acquire certain objects or not to engage in certain activities.
6. The court may impose other duties not provided for in the criminal law on a person at his or her request or at the request of other participants in criminal proceedings, as well as at its own discretion, which, in the court's opinion, should have a positive effect on the convicted person's behavior.

7. When imposing criminal impact measures provided for in Chapter IX of this Code and (or) duties provided for in parts 5 and 6 of this Article, the court determines the time within which the convicted person must execute them.
8. When imposing an arrest or imprisonment sentence and not postponing the execution of the sentence in the manner established in this Article, the court must justify its decision.
9. The procedure and conditions for execution of postponement of execution of sentence are established by the Law of the Republic of Lithuania on Probation.

Article. Release from Punishment Due to Illness

1.	A person who has committed a criminal act may be released from punishment if, before the adoption of the court judgment, he or she contracts a serious incurable disease due to which serving the punishment would be too difficult. In this case, the court, when adopting a conviction judgment, imposes a punishment on such person and releases him or her from serving the punishment. When deciding this issue, the court takes into account the gravity of the criminal act committed, the personality of the convicted person and the nature of the illness.
2.	A person who contracts a serious incurable disease after the adoption of the judgment may be released from further serving of the punishment. When deciding this issue, the court takes into account the gravity of the criminal act committed, the personality of the convicted person, his or her behaviour during the serving of the punishment, the nature of the illness and the time of the punishment served.
3.	A person whose mental state becomes disturbed after the commission of the criminal act or the imposition of the punishment and who, as a result, is unable to understand the essence of his or her actions or to control them, shall be released from further serving of the punishment. When releasing such person from punishment, the court decides on the imposition of compulsory medical measures. If such person recovers, he or she may be sent to continue serving the punishment. In such case, the time during which compulsory medical measures were applied shall be counted towards the term of deprivation of liberty on a day-for-day basis.

Article. Lost force as of 2012-07-01

Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-2314, 2004-07-05, Žin., 2004, No. 108-4030 (2004-07-13)

Article. Amnesty

1.	A person who has committed a criminal act may be released from serving all or part of the punishment
by an amnesty act adopted by the Seimas.
2.	The grounds, conditions and procedure for amnesty shall be established by the amnesty act.

Article. Pardon

1.	A convicted person may be released from serving all or part of the punishment if the President of the Republic grants his or her petition for pardon.
2.	The President of the Republic may grant a convicted person's petition for pardon also in the case where, by agreement with a foreign state, it is sought to return to the Republic of Lithuania a citizen of the Republic of Lithuania who is in that foreign state and who, while acting in the interests of the State of the Republic of Lithuania in a foreign state, has been convicted or is subject to criminal prosecution in that foreign state.
Supplemented by a part of the Article:
No. XIII-2502, 2019-11-07, published in TAR 2019-11-11,
i. k. 2019-18050

3.	The procedure for granting pardon shall be established by the President of the Republic.

CHAPTER XI
SPECIAL FEATURES OF CRIMINAL LIABILITY OF MINORS

Article. Purpose of Special Features of Criminal Liability of Minors

The purpose of the special features of criminal liability of minors provided for in this Chapter and in paragraphs 2 and 3 of Article 13, paragraph 4 of Article 27 and paragraph 4 of Article 97 of this Code is:
1)	to ensure that liability corresponds to the age and social maturity of these persons;
2)	to limit the punishment of deprivation of liberty and to increase the possibilities of applying educational measures to these
persons;
3)	to help the minor to change his or her way of life and behaviour by combining punishment for the criminal act committed with the development of his or her personality, education, and elimination of the causes of unlawful behaviour;
4)	to deter the minor from committing new criminal acts.

Article. Application of the Provisions of the Chapter

1.	The provisions of this Chapter shall apply to persons who had not reached eighteen years of age at the time of commission of the criminal act.
2.	The provisions of Articles 90 to 94 of this Code, as well as the educational measures provided for in points 1, 2, 3 and 5 of paragraph 1 of Article 82, may be applied to a person who had reached eighteen years of age but had not reached twenty-one years of age at the time of commission of the criminal act, if the court, having regard to the nature of the criminal act committed, the motives and other circumstances of the case, and, where necessary, to the explanations or opinion of a specialist, decides that such person is equivalent to a minor in terms of social maturity and that the application of special features of criminal liability to him or her would correspond to the purpose provided for in Article 80 of this Code.

Article. Educational Measures for Minors

1.	The following educational measures may be imposed on a minor who has committed a criminal misdemeanour or a crime and has been released from criminal liability or punishment, as well as on a minor for whom the execution of punishment has been suspended or who has been conditionally released from a deprivation of liberty institution:
Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIV-1199, 2022-06-28, published in TAR 2022-07-15,
i. k. 2022-15557
1)	warning;
2)	compensation for pecuniary damage or its elimination;
3)	unpaid work of an educational nature;
4)	placement with parents or other natural or legal persons who care for children, for upbringing and supervision;
5)	restriction of behaviour;
6)	placement in a special educational institution.
2.	The court may impose on a minor no more than three mutually compatible educational measures.
3.	(lost force).
Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-2314, 2004-07-05, Žin., 2004, No. 108-4030 (2004-07-13)
No. X-1233, 2007-06-28, Žin., 2007, No. 81-3309 (2007-07-21)

Article. Warning

1.	A warning may be imposed on a minor as an independent educational measure or together with other such measures.
2.	The court, when imposing this educational measure on a minor, shall explain in writing the possible legal consequences if he or she were to commit new criminal acts.

Article. Compensation for Pecuniary Damage or Its Elimination

1.	Compensation for pecuniary damage or its elimination shall be imposed only in the case where the minor has funds which he or she independently disposes of, or can eliminate the damage caused by his or her own work.
2.	Pecuniary damage must be compensated or eliminated by one's own work within the time limit set by the court.

Article. Unpaid Work of an Educational Nature

1.	Unpaid work of an educational nature shall be imposed for 20 to 100 hours in health care, social services or other state or non-state institutions and organisations where the work may be of an educational nature.
2.	Unpaid work of an educational nature shall be imposed only in the case where the minor
agrees.
3.	Unpaid work may not be imposed on a minor if he or she is placed in a special
educational institution.
Amendments to the Article:
No. XIII-2263, 2019-06-27, published in TAR 2019-07-05,
i. k. 2019-11177

Article

Placement with Parents or Other Natural or Legal Persons Who Care for Children, for Upbringing and Supervision
1.	Placement with parents or other natural or legal persons who care for children, for upbringing and supervision, shall be established for a period from six months to three years, but not longer than until the minor reaches eighteen years of age.
2.	The measure referred to in paragraph 1 of this Article may be imposed in cases where:
1)	the parents or other persons agree to bring up and supervise the minor, do not themselves have a negative influence on the minor, have the capacity to create appropriate conditions for the development of his or her personality, and agree to provide the necessary information to the institutions controlling the execution of this measure;
2)	the minor agrees to be brought up and supervised by the specified persons and undertakes to obey them and to behave properly.
3.	Placement with parents or other persons for upbringing and supervision may be imposed on a minor as an independent measure or together with other educational measures. This measure may not be imposed if the minor is placed in a special educational institution.

Article. Restriction of Conduct

1.	Conduct may be restricted from thirty days to twelve months. The term of this measure of influence is calculated in days and months.
2.	The court may impose obligations on a minor to:
1)	be at home at a specified time;
2)	study, continue education or work;
3)	acquire certain knowledge or learn prohibitions (road safety, student rules, etc.);
4)	complete a full course of treatment for alcoholism, drug addiction, substance abuse or venereal disease. This obligation is assigned at the request of parents or guardians if the minor consents;
5)	participate in social education or rehabilitation measures organized by state or non-state institutions and organizations.
3.	The court may prohibit a minor from:
1)	playing games of chance;
2)	engaging in certain activities;
3)	driving a motor vehicle (motorcycle, self-propelled machine, etc.);
4)	visiting places that have a negative influence on the minor's conduct, or associating with people who have a negative influence on him or her;
5)	changing place of residence without the knowledge of the institutions controlling the implementation of this measure.
4.	The court may, at the request of the minor or other participants in criminal proceedings, as well as at its own discretion, impose other obligations or prohibitions not provided for in the criminal law which, in the court's opinion, would have a positive influence on the minor's conduct.
5.	The minor must report on the implementation of obligations and prohibitions in the prescribed manner.
6.	Restriction of a minor's conduct may be assigned to a minor as an independent measure of influence or together with other measures of educational influence. This measure may not be assigned if the minor is placed in a special educational institution.
Amendments to the Article:
No. XI-1861, 2011-12-22, Žin., 2012, No. 5-138 (2012-01-07)

Article. Placement in a Special Educational Institution

1.	Placement in a special educational institution is established from six months to three years, but not longer than until the minor reaches eighteen years of age.
2.	The specific period of stay in a special educational institution is determined by the court, taking into account the personality of the minor, whether his or her criminal conduct is repeated, what measures of influence have already been applied, and other circumstances of the case.
3.	Placement in a special educational institution may be assigned to a minor as an independent measure of influence or together with a warning or compensation or removal of property damage.

Article. Consequences of Non-Implementation of Measures of Educational Influence

1.	If a minor to whom one measure of educational influence has been assigned fails to implement it or implements it improperly and has been warned at least twice for this reason, the court, based on the submission of the institutions controlling the implementation of this measure, may replace that measure of influence with any other measure of educational influence, except placement in a special educational institution.
2.	If a minor to whom two or three measures of educational influence have been assigned fails to implement them or implements them improperly and has been warned at least twice for this reason, the court, based on the submission of the institutions controlling the implementation of these measures, may replace them with other measures of educational influence, including placement in a special educational institution.

Article. Peculiarities of Punishments for Minors

1.	Only the following punishments may be imposed on a minor:
1)	community service;
2)	fine;
3)	restriction of liberty;
4)	arrest;
5)	fixed-term imprisonment.
2.	No more than 240 hours of community service may be imposed on minors.
3.	A fine may be imposed only on a working minor or a minor who has his or her own property. A fine of 5 to 50 MGL may be imposed on a minor.
Amendments to the part of the Article:
No. XIII-653, 2017-09-28, published in TAR 2017-10-05,
i. k. 2017-15859

4.	An arrest of five to forty-five days may be imposed on a minor.
5.	A sentence of imprisonment for a minor may not exceed ten years.
Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, No. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)
No. IX-2314, 2004-07-05, Žin., 2004, No. 108-4030 (2004-07-13)

Article. Peculiarities of Imposing a Sentence on a Minor

1.	The court imposes a sentence on a minor guided by the general grounds for imposing sentences and the peculiarities provided for in this chapter.
2.	The court, when imposing a sentence on a minor, in addition to the circumstances listed in paragraph 2 of Article 54 of this Code, takes into account:
1)	the living and upbringing conditions of the minor;
2)	the health condition and social maturity of the minor;
3)	previously applied measures of influence and their effectiveness;
4)	the conduct of the minor after committing the criminal act.
3.	The court may impose a fixed-term imprisonment on a minor if there is reason to believe that other types of punishments are insufficient to change the minor's criminal inclinations, or if the minor has committed a serious or very serious crime. When a sentence of imprisonment is imposed on a minor, its minimum constitutes half of the minimum sentence provided for in the sanction of the article of this Code under which the minor is being tried.
Amendments to the Article:
No. IX-2314, 2004-07-05, Žin., 2004, No. 108-4030 (2004-07-13)

This excerpt sits under: Consolidated version from 2024-01-01

Article. Suspension of Sentence Execution for a Minor

1.	For a minor sentenced to arrest, the court may suspend the execution of the imposed sentence from one month to nine months and impose intensive supervision on them – control of the minor's whereabouts according to a set schedule by means of electronic monitoring. The minor's whereabouts and time are determined by the court. Together with intensive supervision, the court may impose on the minor one or several mutually compatible educational measures provided for in Article 82 of this Code, except placement in a special educational institution, and set the time within which the minor must fulfil them.
2.	For a minor sentenced to imprisonment for one or several negligent crimes or to imprisonment for not more than five years for one or several intentional crimes, the court may suspend the execution of the imposed sentence from one to three years. The execution of the sentence may be suspended if the court decides that there is sufficient ground to believe that the purposes of the sentence will be achieved without actual serving of the sentence.
3.	For a minor sentenced to more than five years for one or several intentional crimes, except very serious crimes and serious crimes provided for in Chapters XV, XVI, XVII, XVIII, XX, XXI, XXIII, XXXIII, XXXV, XXXVII, XLIV of this Code, the court may partially suspend the execution of the imposed sentence. When partially suspending the execution of the sentence, the court determines the part of the imprisonment sentence to be served immediately after the entry into force of the court judgment, which may not be shorter than six months and longer than one year, as well as the term of suspension of execution of the remaining unserved sentence from one to three years.
4.	The execution of the sentence under paragraphs 1, 2 and 3 of this Article may be suspended if the court decides that there is sufficient ground to believe that the purposes of the sentence will be achieved without actual serving of the sentence or after the person has served only part of it.
5.	When suspending the execution of the sentence under paragraphs 2 and 3 of this Article, the court imposes on the minor one or several mutually compatible educational measures provided for in Article 82 of this Code, except placement in a special educational institution. At the same time, the court sets the time within which the minor must fulfil the educational measures.
6.	When imposing an arrest or imprisonment sentence and not suspending the execution of the sentence in the manner established in this Article, the court must give reasons for its decision.
7.	The procedure and conditions for the execution of suspension of sentence execution for minors are established by the Law of the Republic of Lithuania on Probation.
Amendments to the Article:

Article. Release of a Minor from Criminal Liability

1.	A minor who has committed a criminal misdemeanour or a negligent or minor or medium gravity intentional crime for the first time may be released from criminal liability by the court if they:
1)	apologised to the injured person and fully or partially compensated or remedied the property damage caused by their work or money or
2)	has been recognised as having limited culpability, or
3)	admitted their guilt and regrets having committed the criminal act or there are other grounds to believe that the minor will comply with the laws and will not commit new criminal acts.
2.	The court, having released the minor from criminal liability on the grounds provided for in paragraph 1 of this Article, imposes on them the educational measures provided for in Article 82 of this Code.

XII	CHAPTER
STATUTE OF LIMITATIONS FOR CRIMINAL LIABILITY

Article. Statute of Limitations for Conviction

1.	A person who has committed a criminal act may not be convicted if:
1)	the following periods have elapsed:
a)	three years since a criminal misdemeanour was committed;
b)	eight years since a negligent or minor intentional crime was committed;
c)	twelve years since a moderately serious intentional crime was committed;
d)	fifteen years since a serious crime was committed;
e)	twenty-five years since a very serious crime was committed;
f)	thirty years since a crime involving the intentional taking of another person's life was committed;
2)	during the period specified in point 1 of paragraph 1 of this Article, the person did not evade pre-trial investigation or court proceedings and did not commit a new intentional criminal act.
2.	The limitation period is calculated from the commission of the criminal act until the date of conviction.
3.	If a minor was a victim of criminal acts provided for in Chapters XVIII, XX, XXI, XXIII and XLIV of this Code, the limitation period may not expire before that person reaches the age of twenty-five.
4.	If a person who has committed a criminal act evades pre-trial investigation or court proceedings, the running of the limitation period is suspended. The running of the limitation period resumes from the day on which the person is detained or on which he or she voluntarily appears before a pre-trial investigation officer, prosecutor or court. However, a conviction may not be entered if twenty-five years have elapsed since the person committed the criminal act, or thirty years since the commission of a crime involving the intentional taking of another person's life, and the running of the limitation period has not been interrupted by the commission of a new intentional criminal act.
5.	If a person who has committed a criminal act has immunity from criminal jurisdiction under the laws of the Republic of Lithuania or norms of international law and permission has not been obtained from a competent authority to prosecute him or her, the running of the limitation period is suspended. The running of the limitation period resumes from the day on which permission is obtained from a competent authority to prosecute the person who committed the criminal act or he or she otherwise loses the immunity referred to in this paragraph.
6.	During the trial, the running of the limitation period is suspended for the period during which:
1)	the court declares a recess in the trial or postpones the trial due to the non-attendance of the accused or his or her defence counsel;
2)	the court declares a recess in the trial until an expert examination or specialist examination ordered by the court is carried out or a request for legal assistance to a foreign state is executed;
3)	the court declares a recess in the trial and instructs the prosecutor or pre-trial investigation judge to carry out procedural actions provided for in the Code of Criminal Procedure of the Republic of Lithuania;
4)	the court declares a recess in the trial for a newly appointed defence counsel of the accused to familiarise himself or herself with the case materials.
7.	In the cases provided for in paragraph 5 of this Article, a conviction may not be entered if a period five years longer than that provided for in paragraph 1 of this Article has elapsed from the beginning of the limitation period.
8.	If a person commits a new intentional criminal act before the expiry of the periods specified in this Article, the running of the limitation period is interrupted. In this case, the running of the limitation period for the first criminal act begins from the day on which the new intentional crime or criminal misdemeanour was committed.
9.	There is no statute of limitations for the following crimes provided for in this Code:
1)	genocide (Article 99);
2)	internationally prohibited treatment of persons (Article 100);
3)	enforced disappearance (Article 1001);
4)	torture (Article 1003);
Supplemented by Article point:

Article. Statute of Limitations for Enforcement of Conviction

1.	A conviction shall not be enforced if:
1)	it has not been enforced:
a)	within two years where a sentence has been imposed for a criminal misdemeanour, or
b)	within three years where a non-custodial sentence has been imposed, or where a custodial sentence not exceeding two years has been imposed, or
c)	within five years where a custodial sentence not exceeding five years has been imposed, or
d)	within ten years where a custodial sentence not exceeding ten years has been imposed, or
e)	within fifteen years where a custodial sentence exceeding ten years or life imprisonment has been imposed, and

2)	during the period specified in point 1 of paragraph 1 of this Article, the convicted person did not evade serving the imposed sentence and did not commit a new criminal act.
2.	The limitation period for enforcement of a conviction is calculated from the date on which the judgment becomes final until the commencement of enforcement of the judgment.
3.	If the convicted person evades serving the sentence after the judgment becomes final, the running of the limitation period is suspended. In this case, it resumes from the day on which the convicted person voluntarily appears to serve the sentence or is detained. However, the judgment may not be enforced if fifteen years have elapsed since the date on which the judgment became final, or twenty years where imprisonment for more than ten years or life imprisonment has been imposed, and the running of the limitation period has not been interrupted by the commission of a new criminal act.
4.	If the convicted person commits a new criminal act before the expiry of the limitation period for enforcement of the conviction, the running of the limitation period is interrupted. In this case, the limitation period for enforcement of the conviction begins from the commission of the new crime or criminal misdemeanour.
5.	When imposing a sentence for a new criminal act, the court shall follow the rules established in Article 64 of this Code.
CHAPTER XIII CRIMINAL RECORD

Article. Criminal Record

1. Persons convicted of committing a crime against whom a conviction judgment of a court of the Republic of Lithuania or another European Union Member State has entered into force shall be considered to have a criminal record. Persons convicted of committing a crime in a non-European Union Member State shall also be considered to have a criminal record if, on the basis of international treaties of the Republic of Lithuania, information has been received that a conviction judgment of a court of that state has entered into force against them. The court shall take into account the criminal record when imposing a sentence for the commission of a new criminal act, when deciding on the release of the offender from punishment or criminal liability, and also when recognising a person as a dangerous recidivist.
2. Taking into account a criminal record, only those rights and freedoms of citizens may be restricted the limitation of which is provided for by the laws of the Republic of Lithuania.
3. The following shall be considered to have a criminal record:
1) persons for whom the execution of the sentence was suspended – during the period for which the execution of the sentence was suspended;
2) persons convicted of negligent crimes – during the period of serving the sentence;
3) persons convicted of intentional crimes who have actually served the imposed sentence – during the period of serving the sentence and after serving the sentence or release from serving the sentence:
a) three years if they were convicted of a minor or less serious crime;
b) five years if they were convicted of a serious crime;
c) eight years if they were convicted of a very serious crime;
d) ten years if they are dangerous recidivists or if, in the case referred to in paragraph 2 of Article 51 of this Code, a sentence of deprivation of liberty for life was replaced by a sentence of deprivation of liberty for a fixed term.
Amendments to the paragraph of the Article:
No. XIII-2005, 2019-03-21, published TAR 2019-04-02,
i. k. 2019-05331

4. For minors convicted of the crimes provided for in point 3 of paragraph 3 of this Article, the terms of criminal record after serving the sentence or release from serving the sentence shall be reduced by half.
5. The terms established in point 3 of paragraph 3 and in paragraph 4 of this Article shall be calculated from the serving of the imposed sentence or release from serving the sentence.
6. When the terms established in this Article expire, the criminal record shall be extinguished and the persons shall be considered to have no criminal record.
7. When not less than half of the criminal record term has expired, the court may, at the request of the convicted person, shorten the criminal record period or cancel the criminal record.

8. If a person with a criminal record commits a new crime or criminal misdemeanour, the course of extinguishment of the criminal record shall be interrupted. In this case, the term of extinguishment of the criminal record for the previous criminal act shall begin to be calculated from the serving of the sentence for the new crime or criminal misdemeanour. A person shall be considered to have a criminal record for each criminal act until the criminal record for the most serious of them is extinguished.
9. The court, when imposing a sentence for the commission of a new criminal act, when deciding on the release of the offender from punishment or criminal liability, and also when recognising a person as a dangerous recidivist, shall not take into account a conviction judgment of another state's court referred to in paragraph 1 of this Article that has entered into force if:
1) taking into account the conviction judgment that has entered into force would violate fundamental human rights and (or) freedoms;
2) the act committed is not considered a crime under this Code;
3) the person at the time of committing the criminal act had not reached the age from which criminal liability for the act committed by him is possible under the criminal laws of the Republic of Lithuania;
4) there is insufficient information received about the conviction judgment of another state's court and the state that forwarded this information does not provide it within the established term;
5) there are other grounds established in international treaties of the Republic of Lithuania.

No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, No. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)
No. IX-2314, 2004-07-05, Žin., 2004, No. 108-4030 (2004-07-13)

XIV CHAPTER
COMPULSORY MEDICAL MEASURES

Article. Compulsory Medical Measures

1. For persons recognised by the court as not criminally responsible or having limited criminal responsibility, as well as for persons whose mental state deteriorated after the commission of a criminal act or the imposition of a sentence and who therefore cannot understand the nature of their actions or control them, the court may apply the following compulsory medical measures:
1) outpatient observation in primary mental health care conditions;
2) inpatient observation in general observation conditions in specialised mental health care institutions;
3) inpatient observation in enhanced observation conditions in specialised mental health care institutions;
4) inpatient observation in strict observation conditions in specialised mental health care institutions.
2. The court shall apply outpatient observation to a person who, due to the dangerousness of the act committed and his mental disorder, does not need to be observed and treated in an inpatient facility or who can continue outpatient treatment when his mental state improves after inpatient treatment.
3. The court shall apply inpatient observation in general observation conditions to a person who, due to his mental disorder, needs to be observed and treated in a specialised inpatient facility.
4. The court shall apply inpatient observation in enhanced observation conditions to a person who, due to the dangerousness of the act committed and his mental disorder, needs to be observed and treated in a specialised inpatient facility.
5. The court shall apply inpatient observation in strict observation conditions to a person who attempted against human life or health, who due to mental disorder is particularly dangerous to others and must be observed and treated in a specialised inpatient facility.
6. The court shall not establish the duration of application of compulsory medical measures. They shall be applied until the person recovers or his mental state improves and his dangerousness disappears. The court must, not less frequently than once every six months, on the basis of a conclusion of a health care institution, decide on the question of extending the application of compulsory medical measures, changing their type or cancelling their application.
7. If it is not necessary to apply compulsory medical measures to a person, and also if the court cancels the application of these measures, the person may be transferred by the court to the care or custody of relatives or other persons and at the same time medical observation may be established for him.

Amendment to the Article:
No. X-970, 2006-12-12, Žin., 2006, No. 141-5401 (2006-12-28)
SPECIAL PART XV CHAPTER
CRIMES AGAINST HUMANITY AND WAR CRIMES

Article. Genocide

A person who, with the aim of physically destroying all or part of people belonging to any national, ethnic, racial, religious, social or political group, organised, led or participated in killing them, torturing them, injuring them, disrupting their mental development, deporting them, otherwise creating such living conditions that they resulted in the death of all or part of them, restricted the birth rate of people belonging to those groups or forcibly transferred their children to other groups,
shall be punished by deprivation of liberty for a term of five to twenty years or by deprivation of liberty for life.

This excerpt sits under: Consolidated version from 2024-01-01

straipsnis. Conduct towards persons prohibited by international law

Any person who, intentionally, in the course of implementing or supporting a policy of a state or organisation to attack civilians on a large scale or systematically, killed them or seriously impaired their health; created such living conditions that they resulted in the death of people; enslaved people; deported or forcibly transferred populations; unlawfully imprisoned or otherwise restricted the physical liberty of persons in violation of international law; tortured; raped or sexually abused persons, forced them into sexual slavery or forced them to engage in prostitution; unlawfully deprived a forcibly impregnated woman of liberty with the aim of changing the ethnic composition of the population or committing another violation of international law; forcibly sterilised persons or committed other acts of sexual violence of a similar nature; persecuted any group or community of persons on political, racial, national, ethnic, cultural, religious, gender or other grounds prohibited by international law; detained, arrested, abducted or otherwise deprived persons of their liberty where such deprivation of liberty is not recognised, or failed to report on the fate or whereabouts of persons; practised apartheid,
shall be punished by imprisonment for a term of five to twenty years or by imprisonment for life.
Amendments to the Article:
No. XI-1291, 2011-03-22, Žin., 2011, No. 38-1805 (2011-03-31)
1001 straipsnis. Enforced disappearance
Any person who, acting as a representative of the state or a person or group of persons acting with the permission, support or consent of the state, detained, arrested, abducted a person or otherwise deprived him of liberty, where there is a refusal to acknowledge such detention, arrest, abduction or deprivation of liberty, or concealed the fate or whereabouts of the disappeared person,
shall be punished by imprisonment for a term of three to fifteen years.

1002 straipsnis. Separation of children
Any person who unlawfully separated children, knowing that those children, their parents or guardians suffered from the criminal acts provided for in Article 1001 of this Code,
shall be punished by imprisonment for a term of up to eight years.

1003 straipsnis. Torture
1.	Any person who, in implementing state policy or acting with the permission, instigation, support or consent of the state, tortured or otherwise treated a person with extreme cruelty in any manner, thereby causing the victim severe physical or mental pain or great suffering,

shall be punished by imprisonment for a term of up to seven years.

straipsnis. Killing of persons protected by international humanitarian law

Any person who, during war, armed conflict, aggression, occupation or annexation, ordered, incited or organised the killing of, or killed, persons protected by international humanitarian law,
shall be punished by imprisonment for a term of ten to twenty years or by imprisonment for life.
Amendments to the Article:
No. XI-1291, 2011-03-22, Žin., 2011, No. 38-1805 (2011-03-31)

straipsnis. Deportation or transfer of civilians

Any person who, during war, armed conflict or during aggression, occupation or annexation, ordered, incited or organised the deportation of, or deported, civilian population from occupied or annexed territory to the territory of the occupying or annexing state or a third state; ordered, incited or organised the transfer or forcible expulsion of, or transferred or forcibly expelled, the civilian population of an occupied or annexed state within the territory of that state in violation of international law; ordered, incited or organised the transfer of, or transferred, the civilian population of the occupying state to the territory of the occupied country,
shall be punished by imprisonment for a term of five to fifteen years.

No. IX-1495, 2003-04-10, Žin., 2003, No. 38-1733 (2003-04-24)
